金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 7 月 27 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 川普關稅政策再掀波瀾：川普政府於 7/23 宣布替代版本,將於 7/24 零時生效,對美國主要
貿易夥伴課徵 10%–12.5% 稅率,涵蓋美國逾 99.4% 的進口品項;7/20 川普另依 1930 年
關稅法 Section 338 簽署三份公告,對加拿大酒類、曲棍球棍、水泥等特定商品加徵 50%
額外關稅,並同步啟動鋁冶煉廠投資獎勵計畫。
◼ ECB 利率按兵不動，委員關注中東能源二輪效應： ECB 於今年 6 月才因中東衝突與能源價

格壓力宣布升息一碼，7 月採「鷹派按兵不動」策略。理事會聲明強調當前能源價格「仍高
度波動、遠高於中東衝突前水位」 、且能源衝擊對通膨的完整二輪效應「尚未完全顯現」 。市
場當日對 9/10 ECB 再度升息機率定價約 70%。
◼ 胡塞攻擊推升油價，美伊暫停交火後市場降溫，但風險仍未根除：胡塞武裝 7/23 攻擊兩艘
沙國油輪，25 日再襲擊沙國紅海沿岸石油設施，推升能源運輸中斷疑慮。惟美伊 25 日至 26
日暫停相互攻擊，27 日亞洲盤油價回落逾 5%，美債殖利率亦降至 4.63%。但荷莫茲海峽與
紅海航運仍受阻，地緣政治溢價尚未完全消退。
市場展望與操作策略
◼ AI 支出與油價上揚引發疑慮，惟基本面料可支撐股市前景：上週 Alphabet 與 Tesla 財報引發
市場疑慮，中東局勢發展亦加重通膨與股市壓力。然而，台積電 Q2 營收達財測上緣，並將全
年美元營收成長展望上調至略高於 40%，強調 AI 需求依舊強勁；此外，Google Cloud 未交
付訂單增至 5,140 億美元，顯示 AI 前景仍具支撐。本波修正較偏向高估值與資金面的重新定

價，尚非 AI 基本面反轉，建議利用拉回分批布局具獲利潛力的科技股及台、日股市。
◼ 3~10 年美國公債/投資級債為債市首選：隨著 3～10 年期殖利率同步回升至具吸引力水準，
現階段可望兼具較高債息收益與適度存續期間效益 ； 債券天期過短面臨再投資風險 ， 超長天期
債又較易受財政赤字、公債供給及期限溢酬影響，建議持續以 3～10 年期美國公債搭配投資
級債，兼顧收益、防禦與未來利率回落時的資本利得機會。

戰火影響料屬短期，無礙市場中期走勢
2026 年 4 月
美國對伊朗展開襲擊所引發的中東戰火延續至今，拖累 3 月截至 27 日的全球金融股市表現，
市場在多重變數交織下動能顯著受壓，主要股市指數單月報酬全盤皆墨。不過，在能源類股大漲的支
撐下，S&P 500 指數表現相對其他股市穩定。日本股市雖亦出現獲利了結壓力，但受惠於高市政府
「負責且積極的財政政策」 及日圓匯率弱勢有利出口 ， 今年以來 MSCI 日本指數的表現僅次於拉丁美
洲股市。另一方面，受油價高漲帶動通膨預期、外資持續撤出且經濟放緩所累，印度股市出現賣壓，

MSCI 印度指數不論在 3 月或是今年至今表現都是敬陪末座。
全球主要債市 3 月報酬亦全數下跌。在所有券種之中 ， 以新興當地債下跌 5.21% 表現最為疲弱 ，
除了油價引發市場對於進口原油的新興國家前景的擔憂外 ，高漲的美元指數也使得當地貨幣計價新興
債表現呈壓。 全球主權債同樣因為油價高漲推升通膨疑慮 ，投資人對於各國央行貨幣政策或因而轉為
更為鷹派高度警戒，使其表現差強人意。
自去年川普關稅「解放日」後美股跌至低點後開始走揚，各國股市與主要指數持續上揚，其中又
以金礦指數、南韓股市、費半指數、日本股市表現最佳。然而，自中東戰事 2/28 爆發後，以指數修
正幅度來看，跌幅最大的市場分別為金礦指數、南韓股市、日本股市。唯有費半指數則在 AI 需求暢
旺的加持下成為例外。由此可見，本波市場的回跌，基本上為「漲多跌多」的修正效應。
就基本面來看，2026 年美股企業獲利增長主軸仍在 AI， 由於2026 年仍在 AI 基礎建設期擴張間 ，
AI 終端需求仍是科技龍頭企業，故所帶動的獲利增長料不致受油價影響，今年單季盈餘增長可望維

持 2 位數水準。自中東戰事以來，彭博對於 MSCI 美國指數 Q1 及 Q2 的 EPS 預期不跌反漲，顯示市
場對於美國企業的獲利前景仍極具信心。同時，目前的資訊科技類股的預期 P/E 比與過去 10 年的均
值相較仍過有遭到低估之嫌，也預示科技類股相較於其他類股仍有進一步向上的動能。
再者，儘管近期美國公債殖利率因市場的通膨疑慮而上揚 ， 但目前殖利率遠高於Fed 長期通膨目
標，投資人仍可以資產配置角度對於債券作適當之配置。考量短期殖利率受市場對 Fed 的利率政策
預期變動影響較大，故在年期與券種的選擇上可以 7~15 年的中長期美國公債或投資級債為主。

美國股市：標普 500 企業盈餘增長加速，私募信貸贖回潮不致衝擊美國經濟
美國第四季 GDP 季增年率修正值 0.7%，較初值 1.4%有所調降，主要反映實質個人消費季比降
至 0.7%。第四季 GDP 數據整體仍支持美國經濟穩健。但第一季受通膨(關稅與伊朗危機)與高利率影
響，消費行為呈現「降級消費」特徵，非必需性支出收縮，經濟呈現倚賴「高收入族群支撐消費」的
K 型分化格局。由於中東戰火推高能源價格，美國民眾擔心生活成本壓力惡化，美國密西根大學3 月
消費者信心指數降至 55.5，較 2 月終值 56.6 下降 1.1，觸及今年以來最低紀錄，反映中東危機與生

活成本壓力削弱民眾樂觀情緒。
2 月非農就業人數淨減少 9.2 萬個，低於彭博預期的增加 1.3 萬，失業率躍升至 4.4%，失業率雖
短期上揚、但就長期角度仍在低檔。追究原因數據主要是受到罷工和天氣等短期因素干擾，導致醫療
健保、建築業、零售業和休閒酒店等產業表現不佳，但整體勞動市場狀態溫和，從請領就業金人數等
數據看來就業市場尚未出現顯著惡化跡象。市場先前擔憂 AI 取代人力，目前數據看來企業僅是招募
保守，後續可觀察勞動市場降溫是否反映在薪資成長，若薪資壓力下降有助於抑制服務業通膨，除直
接改善企業成本結構外，也將使聯準會擔憂的服務業通膨出現放緩。
預估第一季標普 500 企業餘年增仍可達到 11.3%，且在第二季顯著拉升至近兩成水準，科技板
塊受惠於 AI 仍是帶動美股企業獲利增長主軸。大型科技巨擘透過AI 優化流程，開始對利潤率產生正
面影響。然而，此一效益目前高度集中於資本與技術優勢明顯的科技龍頭企業，中小企業與非科技產
業尚未顯著受益。因此，企業獲利將呈現明顯分化格局，而尚未受惠於 AI 的非科技業者，將會在未

來幾年逐步提高使用 AI 程度，傳統企業大規模導入 AI 模型與使用算力，將可成為 AI 龍頭下一波業
績成長動能。
近期美國私募信貸市場爆發贖回潮，包含貝萊德、阿波羅、藍鷹(Blue Owl)等巨頭均遭投資人要
求提款，甚至限制贖回以防止流動性枯竭。此波動盪主因投資人擔憂 AI 技術衝擊軟體業導致違約風
險上升，以及對高收益資產的獲利了結。研判此類事件雖造成金融股拉回，但引發總體系統性危機的
可能性極低。
目前標普 500 本益比估計在 22.84 倍、股價淨值比 4.95 倍，短期評價偏高，面對伊朗戰事的干
擾，預料股市震盪加劇，但若市場拉回，基於美國在 AI 產業具備絕對優勢，仍建議以長線角度布局。

歐洲股市：歐元區經濟表現穩健，央行面對能源危機採取警戒態度
歐元區第四季 GDP 季增率自初值的 0.3%下修至 0.2%，年增則下修至 1.2%，二者皆低於市場預
期。歐元區第四季經濟動能略微放緩，成長主要由內需帶動。家庭消費在穩健的勞動力市場與薪資增
長支撐下，季增率上升至 0.2%，固定資本形成雖由高點回落，但仍維持正成長，外部需求疲軟導致
出口季減，加上企業去庫存，顯示內需支撐正與貿易逆風強烈拉鋸。
三月份歐元區經濟活動連續 15 個月處於擴張區間，其中製造業表現強勁，歐元區製造業PMI 由
50.8 上升至 51.4，創 45 個月高點，受新訂單增長支撐，惟出口訂單連續第 49 個月低於榮枯線，外

需疲軟仍未改善。服務業受中東戰爭引發的不確定性與能源成本飆升衝擊，驟降至 50.1，放緩至 10
個月低點。預料南歐以觀光業為主國家將面對能源成本衝擊，而外銷為主的如德國、法國將迎來能源
與運輸成本飆升的雙重打擊。歐盟決定延後提交原定 4/15 提出將俄羅斯石油進口永久禁令入法的提
案，自 2022 年俄羅斯全面入侵烏克蘭以來，歐盟已大幅降低對俄能源依賴，是否因為伊朗戰爭調整
制裁內容將是未來關注重點。
歐洲央行在 3 月利率決策會議決定利率不變、但態度轉鷹。歐洲央行總裁拉加德表示，若通膨出
現「顯著但不過於持久」的超標情況，可能進行「適度的政策調整」，以確保通膨預期維持穩定。她
強調 ， 若對通膨偏離目標完全不作反應 ， 恐影響政策溝通與市場信心 ， 使外界難以理解央行反應機制 。
從利率期貨觀察 ， 市場預期歐洲央行與英國央行都有機會在第二季升息1 碼 ， 不排除未來一年升息到
2 碼，顯示歐洲央行與英國央行面對能源危機採取警戒態度。
在地緣風險常態化背景下，歐洲各國持續提高國防支出，德國政府規劃在2026 年投入 380 億歐

元於基礎建設、氣候保護專項基金、國防及數位化，加上 AI 浪潮帶動企業投資，投資者原本對歐洲
經濟抱持樂觀態度，然而油價大漲大幅扭轉市場樂觀預期。 考量市場目前悲觀原因仍是地緣政治風
險，假設美伊衝突能在第二季落幕，對歐股企業獲利影響有限，歐洲經濟將在下半年重回成長軌道。
泛歐 600 指數目前本益比為 16.14，故價淨值比為 2.27。就評價角度相對合理，雖然年初以來歐
股的樂觀情緒因為伊朗戰爭快速扭轉，但未來若油價回落，或是歐洲重新考慮向俄羅斯購買能源，都
能夠化解歐洲面臨的能源困境，故仍可視為長線佈局標的。

日本股市：高市政府加大戰略產業投資，政府補貼壓抑油價衝擊
日本受惠於企業強勁支出 ，2025 年第四季 GDP 年化季增率為 1.3%， 優於原先預估的0.2%。2026
年不僅企業資本支出動能持續 ， 在高市早苗出任首相後 ， 政府選定支持包含實體人工智慧 、 再生醫學 、
量子運算與海洋無人機等 61 個項目，目標在 2040 年前取得全球 AI 機器人逾 30%的市占率，國產
晶片銷售額也要在同年衝上 40 兆日圓。高市希望藉由政府與民間的合作推動投資，強化發展包含人
工智慧、半導體、宇宙及航太等 17 大重點產業。由於自民黨已在國會選舉取得空前壓倒性勝利，產

業補貼計畫將可落實，讓政府投資不僅貢獻短期 GDP，亦可提升日本長期經濟競爭力。
日本 2 月剔除生鮮食品的消費者價格年比上漲 1.6%，創 2022 年 3 月以來最小升幅，主要因為
公用事業補貼壓低能源成本。日本勞動組合總連合會(連合)近期公布 2026 年春鬥第 1 次彙整結果，
加薪幅度雖低於去年同期 、 不過已連3 年高於 5%，且2026 年中小企業加薪幅度可望高於過去幾年，
支撐日本薪資物價形成正向循環，並達成實質薪資增長。近期伊朗戰事導致油價飆升，首相高市早苗
積極應對，除釋出戰備儲油外，將控制汽油價格上限，故預料短期通膨不致大幅躍昇。
日本央行三月利率決策會議維持政策不變，主要考量來自中東局勢不確定性，難以衡量伊朗戰爭
及國際油價飆漲對日本經濟及通膨帶來影響 ，呼應日本央行總裁植田和男論點 ， 希望有更多時間可以
觀察對物價的引導。後市央行升息時點須留意4 月初將公布的短觀報告，若企業信心尚維持正面，搭
配伊朗戰爭如白宮所預期的在 5、6 周之內結束，則日本央行或有望在第二季升息一碼。預料升息進

度放緩將使十年期日本公債殖利率在 2.0~2.5%區間震盪，且由於全球央行方向轉鷹、美元受惠於避
險需求，日本央行緩步升息有助日圓匯率穩定。
波克夏將對東京海上進行出資 ， 在4 月藉由收購東京海上持有的自家庫藏股 、 取得東京海上2.49%
股權，出資額為 2,874 億日圓。在巴菲特掌舵時期，波克夏對日本企業的投資以大型商社為主，此次
將是波克夏首度和日本金融機構進行合作 ， 性質與過去投資商社股不同 ， 若波克夏未來對日投資可擴
及其他非商社股，對於日股長期表現將帶來更大力度的加持。
2026 年以來日股受惠於高市擴大財政刺激 、強化軍事安全、鞏固美日同盟上，但本益比仍在 18.38
倍，股價淨值比在 1.68 倍。3 月下旬外資賣超日股，估計是受到伊朗戰事干擾且避免股利發放的課
稅而調整持股部位所致。近期日圓震盪走貶，有利於於日本外銷股財報表現，預料企業獲利可望為日
股在全球金融市場震盪下帶來支撐。

新興亞洲股市：中東戰事對經濟影響負面，但成長仍具韌性
亞洲股市 3 月因中東情勢緊張而大跌，截至 3/27，亞洲不含日本指數 (MXAPJ) 單月下跌
10.39%。南韓股市跌幅居前，單月下跌 12.90%，多次於盤中啟動熔斷機制，除了中東地緣政治風
險之外，漲幅過大後的技術性修正也是主因。台股在內資支撐下，跌幅相對較輕，單月下跌 6.50%。
中國股市也表現弱勢，上證指數下跌 5.99%，國企指數下跌 4.58%。3 月份正值全國兩會結束，股市
出現了「利多出盡」的調節。MSCI 東協指數 3 月下跌 3.53%，是跌幅較輕的亞洲區域。馬來西亞與

印尼身為原油、液化天然氣（LNG）及棕櫚油的重要出口國，油價上漲直接帶動其相關權值股走強，
抵銷了跌幅，但越南、菲律賓等原油進口國，跌幅超過 10%。印度 SENSEX 指數下跌 9.48%，印度
是全球第三大石油進口國，約 85% 的原油依賴進口，油價高漲對經濟造衝擊，另外外資於三月大量
賣出，也是印度股市重挫的原因。
亞洲經濟在 2026 年 Q2 將面對能源價格的劇烈波動對經濟的衝擊。根據 ADB 資料，泰國、
南韓與新加坡及印度等國，其能源進口依賴度顯著高於區域平均 。在當前中東衝突陰影未散的背景
下，油價的每一次跳動都觸發亞洲通膨的扳機，對於這些原油高依賴進口國家而言，能源成本的上升
不僅僅是物價問題，更是對 GDP 增長動能的直接侵蝕。
根據野村的推估「油價每上漲 10%」對各國經濟的衝擊路徑各不相同，印度與菲律賓的 CPI 通
膨壓力最為顯著，預計分別跳升 0.5 個百分點，這將迫使兩國央行在 Q2 維持較高利率環境，壓縮
降息空間 。反觀馬來西亞與印尼，受惠於其資源生產國的地位，GDP 增長甚至可能出現微幅的正向

回饋或較小的負面衝擊 。這種「能源非對稱性」將導致亞洲內部經濟與貨幣政策的進一步分化。
然而亞洲經濟在 2026 年 Q2 面臨增長放緩的挑戰。但根據博鰲亞洲論壇報告表示，受國際貿易不
確定性及地緣政治風險上升影響，今年亞洲增長率預計從去年的 4.7% 降至 4.5%。儘管增速微降，
但亞洲經濟體 GDP 占世界比重持續上升，依然是全球經濟的主要成長引擎。這與 ADB 資料相呼
應，顯示亞洲在全球經濟增速放緩的大環境下，憑藉區域一體化進程展現出較佳的韌性 。
對於印度與菲律賓等高通膨敏感區，應關注其內需韌性能否抵銷能源稅的負面影響；而對於新加
坡、南韓與台灣等雖然進口佔比較高但產業鏈地位穩固的經濟體，仍看好在全球 AI 供應鏈帶來的快
速增長。整體而言亞洲國家在 2026 年的經濟增長整體保持韌性，但將出現明確分化。

中國股市：轉型陣痛與外部地緣風險的夾擊
2026 年中國兩會不僅發佈經濟目標，更是「十五五規劃」首年的轉型宣言。官方將 GDP 成長
目標設定在 4.5% 至 5.0% 的區間，這項數據釋放了一個明確信號，北京已不再盲目追求過往的高
速增長，而是將重心置於「新質生產力」的質變與落地。在政策宣示中，我們觀察到由「總量擴張」
向 「結構優化」 的顯著移轉。2026 年數位經濟核心產業佔 GDP 比重目標提升至 12.5%，重點布局
人工智能（AI Agent）與具身智能（Physical AI）的工業化應用。同時，政府推動的大規模「設備更

新與消費品以舊換新」 政策，試圖在傳統房產動能消退之際，透過激發存量市場的替代需求來維持內
需熱度。這種以新經濟與高品質消費為主的政策組合，旨在對沖房地產下行對內需產生的結構性缺
口，並賦予國家經濟轉型的重任。
中國經濟正經歷一場歷史性的動能切換，長期作為增長引擎的房地產，其最終需求對 GDP 的佔
比正持續回落。與此同時，以醫藥、先進設備、IT 通訊及研發為首的「高科技 + 新三樣」產業，其
貢獻權重已經在 2025 年正式超越房地產。轉型過程雖然伴隨著陣痛，卻是中國邁向科技自主的必要
路徑 。 儘管房地產的長尾效應仍在消化 ， 但這些新興領域的快速擴張正為中國經濟建立更具韌性的基
礎，成為觀察中國能否維持區域增速預期的核心支柱。對於投資者而言，這意味著舊有的投資邏輯已
徹底改變，未來市場的超額收益將高度集中在具備全球競爭力的先進製造龍頭。
儘管新經濟轉型積極，但房地產市場的下滑壓力依然是中國最沉重的負擔。根據彭博模型，房地
產尋底之路極為漫長。預測路徑顯示，未來幾年房地產佔 GDP 比重將持續收縮至 12% 左右。中

國的政策博弈重點在於政府如何透過擴張性財政手段來進行 「止血」 ，防止房市超跌引發系統性金融
風險。但在目前市場信心修復極為緩慢的背景下，觀察銷售數據能否在超跌區間出現初步止穩。
然而中國面臨的外部環境更加嚴峻。中東危機的突然升溫，成為干擾復甦的關鍵變數。地緣震盪
推升了能源溢價，加劇輸入型通膨壓力，美國聯準會（Fed）態度的轉向鷹派與美元持續走強，顯著
壓縮了中國央行的降息空間。在強美元與高利差的雙重擠壓下，人民幣匯率面臨巨大的貶值壓力。這
種「內需待援、外部受限」的政策困局，使得操作空間顯得倍加受限，雖然兩會釋放了擴張性財政的
積極信號，但在地緣政治風險與強勢美元背景下，市場缺乏強勁的資金流入動能，也因此我們認為中
國股市欠缺全面性上漲的機會。類股建議在配置上規避傳統房地產產業，鎖定受惠於新質生產力政
策、具備技術護城河且能抵禦外部波動的科技領先標的。

印度股市： 中東戰事與 Fed 轉鷹影響印度成長動能
印度的實體經濟活動成長發出趨緩信號。3 月份最新發布的 HSBC Flash 印度綜合 PMI 指數由 2
月的 58.9 降至 56.5，創下 2022 年 10 月以來最弱的擴張速度，反映出國內需求在物價壓力下正
逐步降溫 。其中，製造業 PMI 降至 53.8，顯示國內訂單成長受阻；然而，值得關注的是國際訂單
卻創下調查歷史新高，這證明在全球供應鏈重組的戰略大環境下，印度製造的外部需求依然堅挺 。
儘管基本面仍具韌性，但 PMI 所揭示的投入成本增速創多年新高，預示著企業利潤空間將面臨嚴峻

的輸入型通膨擠壓，這將是觀察印度經濟能否維持高成長的首要指標。
儘管短期數據出現波動，但印度作為全球增長引擎的核心地位並未動搖。在 2026-27 財政預算
案的架構下， 政府持續推動以基礎設施投資為核心的擴張性政策 ， 試圖透過提升公共支出效率來強化
製造業基礎。印度正積極利用其人口紅利與政策激勵，吸引從電子組裝到半導體測試的全球業者進
駐。然而，這種高度依賴資本流入與外部能源供給的增長模式，在地緣政治動盪與聯準會轉向鷹派的
環境下，正迎來一場壓力測試。「內部政策利多」與「外部環境利空」的互相干擾，使得印度的成長
前景短期面臨不確定性 。
能源價格震盪是印度經濟短期最重要的潛在風險。根據彭博經濟的報導，印度央行估計，原油價
格每上漲 10 美元，經濟成長將會下降 0.15%。若中東衝突導致霍爾木茲海峽關閉三個月，2027 財
年成長率恐重挫至 6.1% 。在極端情況下，燃料成本飆升將引發連鎖反應，不僅削弱消費者的實際收
入，更會因國營石油公司虧損削減股利，進而擠壓預算案中的公共建設支出與政府投資能力。除了價

格波動，供應端的物理性中斷亦是威脅。印度的能源約 85%依賴進口，若航運中斷導致供應缺口，
包含民生消費與石化產業等經濟活動都將受到供應鍊斷裂的風險。
在貨幣政策上 ， 印度央行面臨 「內外夾擊」 。 若油價衝向140 美元 ，CPI 預計將進一步升 5.0%，
並可能在 9 月突破 RBI 的 6%容忍區間，央行被迫在年底臨時升息 2 碼。外部方面，聯準會態度的轉
向鷹派加劇了資金回流美元資產的壓力，也將干擾印度央行的寬鬆節奏。綜合上述分析，我們對印度
市場看法轉向中性。雖然結構性紅利與財政擴張確保了其增長基本盤 ， 但近期的重心已由 「追求擴張」
轉向「防禦通膨與能源衝擊」，這讓 PMI 顯示內需趨緩。加上潛在的油價的大漲與供應減少，正大
幅抵銷供應鏈轉移的長期利多。我們建議在股市配置上採取守勢。在近期的估值修正期，利用市場震
盪，重新評估基本面。

拉美股市：巴西正式啟動降息循環，中東衝突有利拉美經濟
巴西央行貨幣政策委員會(Copom)於 3 月 18 日會議全票通過將基準利率調降 25 個基點至
14.75%，正式啟動新一輪降息循環。Copom 會議紀要強調該決定與當前高度不確定性的背景相符，
在地緣政治衝突持續時間與範圍難以預判 、 經濟活動放緩步調及其對物價水準影響的訊號錯綜複雜之
下，保持從容並收集額外資訊為適當之舉。Copom 也為下次會議將降息步調加速至 50 個基點保
留空間，但也強調需美伊戰爭等外部環境改善且這些事件對通膨與通膨預期影響有限才能達成。

市場通膨預期方面，截至 3 月 30 日當周巴西央行市場調查顯示，2026 年通膨預期上升 14
個基點至 4.31%，高於巴西央行 3.0% 的長期通膨目標中點，2027、2028 及 2029 年則分別為
3.84%、3.57% 及 3.50%，惟有鑑於近期油價持續走升， 預計未來數週通膨預期將可能進一步惡化。
此外，市場對 2026 年底 Selic 利率中位預期維持在 12.50%，2027 及 2028 年底分別為 10.50%
及 10.00%，顯示雖中東戰爭帶動市場通膨升溫，然而巴西市場整體降息格局並未受到太大影響，仍
是朝大幅降息的方向前進。
另一方面，雖美伊戰爭帶動短線油價上漲，帶動拉美市場通膨預期，然而油價上漲事實上也有利
於拉美整體經濟，主因拉美市場中，包含阿根廷、巴西、哥倫比亞與厄瓜多等國為石油淨出口國，高
盛研究指出若油價上漲 10%，能使整體拉美市場未來 12 個月 GDP 上升約 0.2%，顯示美伊戰爭
對拉美市場來說並非全然只有負面影響。
最後一個值得關注的議題是巴西 2026 年的總統大選，市場主要關注現任總統盧拉，與前總統

波索納洛之子弗拉維奧的對決。市調機構 Quaest 3 月民調結果顯示，在第二輪決選中，兩位候選人
首度打成平手，各獲得 41% 支持度，扭轉過往皆由盧拉勝出的局勢，顯示中東衝突引發的能源價格
上漲與通膨壓力持續影響盧拉政權的支持度 。市場預期若最終總統大選由右派陣營的弗拉維奧勝出，
其政策將會有利於巴西財政赤字問題改善，相較於盧拉政營財政擴張的策略 ，更可能吸引外資流入巴
西市場。
展望後市 ，儘管中東衝突帶動拉美市場通膨預期攀升 ，然而拉美市場最大經濟體巴西仍處於降息
循環中，且目前市場並不預期巴西央行會因為能源價格問題，而扭轉寬鬆方向，此外，拉美市場整體
亦會受惠於油價上漲帶動的石油出口助益，故整體而言，我們目前對拉美市場後市仍抱持正向看法。

### Page 9

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
能源類股：美伊停戰協商短期難有結論，可以資產配置角度布局能源類股
美伊衝突仍是 3 月主導能源價格走勢的關鍵，雖然美國在 3 月 25 日透過巴基斯坦向伊朗提出停
戰協議，然而內容與伊朗訴求相差甚遠，預料短期內美伊達成停戰協議的可能性並不高，戰事仍可能
需要來回數次的武力攻擊與談判後，才能底定。
隨戰爭持續，荷莫茲海峽的中斷程度仍是油價最關鍵的變數，戰前荷莫茲海峽每天約有 130 艘
船通過海峽 ， 目前每天僅有不到 6 艘在伊朗許可下通行，海峽運輸仍幾乎處於癱瘓狀態 。 對伊朗而言 ，
封鎖荷莫茲海峽越久 ， 越能為其換得更好的談判籌碼 ， 伊朗國會目前更是在推動立法對通過海峽的船

舶收取過路費，以此將海峽視為經濟收入來源，故何時荷莫茲海峽能夠暢通，仍具高度不確定性。
受荷莫茲海峽運量持續受阻影響，高盛 3 月 23 日報告再度上修油價預測，預估 3 月布蘭特原油
平均價格將達 105 美元，4 月將進一步上升至 115 美元，並在 2026 年第四季回落至 80 美元，該預
測基於荷莫茲海峽石油流量將在 6 週內維持極低水準的假設。此外，在海峽流量中斷 10 週的不利情
境下，高盛預估布蘭特油價將可能接近或超越 2008 年 147 美元的歷史紀錄，隨後在2026 年第四季
放緩至 100 美元；在中斷10 週並同時伴隨基礎設施受損的嚴重不利情境下，油價可能飆至接近160
美元，並在2026 年第四季仍維持 115 美元高油價。在高盛各種情境分析中，若海峽流量中斷時間延
長，或者有更多能源基礎設施受損，長期油價都存在上升的可能性，有利能源類股表現。
另一方面，觀察 Bloomberg 西德州原油期貨遠期曲線，可以看出目前市場仍定價荷莫茲海峽受
阻為中短期事件，曲線在開戰後前端快速上揚，反映現貨端供給緊張狀況，然而遠端合約變動幅度不

大，且近幾個月合約曲線陡峭，顯示市場認為荷莫茲海峽受阻仍將是短期事件，並不預期戰爭將會演
變成長期戰事，預料隨衝突結束，原油供給將大幅恢復，帶動油價下跌。3 月 30 日 WTI 遠期曲線顯
示，油價將會在 11 月回落至每桶 80 美元以下。此外，截至 3/30，國際賭盤 Polymarket 預估美伊
在 4/15 前停戰機率約 17%，4/30 前為 32%，6/30 前為 59%，12/31 前則為 75%， 機率在 4/30
至 6/30 區間增幅最大，隱含市場對停火的預期主要落在 4~6 月。
展望後市，雖然近期美伊皆提出談判條件，然而雙方在談判條件上仍存在顯著落差，再加上對於
伊朗來說，將荷莫茲海峽封鎖的越久，讓能源價格維持高檔，越能為伊朗得到更好的談判籌碼，故預
估伊朗更可能採取邊打邊談的策略，將整體談判時程拉長。對於能源類股來說，短線上若戰爭持續，
能源類股有望走出獨立行情，並可作為投資組合中對衝戰爭風險的角色，而即使在停戰後，長期油價
仍有望高於戰前水準，亦有利支撐能源類股表現，故建議可以資產配置角度，適度布局能源類股。

黃金類股：美伊戰事壓抑短線金價表現，惟反轉條件已逐步成形
黃金傳統上被視為戰爭期間的避險資產，然而本波自 2 月 28 日美伊戰爭爆發以來，金價卻明顯
表現不佳，從戰前約 5,100 美元一路下跌，一度回撤至約 4,100 美元位置。本波金價走疲，主因美伊
戰爭導致荷莫茲海峽受阻，持續推升油價，帶動市場通膨預期上升，同步推高了美國公債殖利率，使
不孳息的黃金資產吸引力下降。此外，市場避險情緒亦帶動美元走強，而黃金價格長期以來與美元走
勢呈負相關，隨美元指數走升，黃金等以美元計價之商品價格亦出現承壓。
此外，黃金重要買盤全球央行，近期購金動能也出現明顯放緩。世界黃金協會報告指出，2026

年 1 月全球央行淨購金量驟降至 5 噸，較去年月均購金量 27 噸銳減近 80%，推測主因 1 月金價波
動過大，導致各國央行態度轉為觀望。 另一方面，土耳其央行近期大幅減持黃金 ，也對市場情緒造
成壓抑。據彭博新聞引述知情人士報導，土耳其央行在 3 月 13 日至 3 月 20 日的兩周內，以出售及
協議交換置換了約 60 噸黃金，用以穩定因美伊戰爭而受影響的土耳其里拉匯率，彭博指出，土耳其
央行本次減持黃金，一部分是採市場直接拋售，一部分則是通過 SWAP 以黃金作為抵押品來獲取外
匯或里拉 ，而歷史上各國央行透過 SWAP 以貴金屬獲得廉價美元融資的情況並不少見，且回顧 2023
年 3 月至 5 月期間，土耳其央行亦曾售出近 160 噸黃金，惟當時因來自黃金 ETF 的買家吸收賣壓，
對短線金價並未造成太大影響，故若僅有來自土耳其央行的一次性賣壓，實際並不需要太過擔憂。
更需要留意的是 ， 中東戰爭將可能迫使其他國家同樣採取出售黃金儲備的動作，以支撐外匯存底
或籌措資金，若此趨勢擴大，將 可能進一步增加黃金短線賣壓， 同時，因目前市場資金較不偏好黃

金，短線上黃金 ETF 吸納來自各國央行賣壓的力道也會較弱，故只要美伊戰爭持續，黃金類股短線
表現將可能受到較大壓力。
即便如此，我們仍可看出世界央行對中長期增持黃金儲備抱持正向態度 ，例如在 1 月份的世界央
行黃金買家中，就意外的出現了馬來西亞央行，為該國自 2018 年以來首度增持黃金儲備，另外在 3
月初，韓國央行也宣布計畫從 2026 年第一季起，將黃金 ETF 納入其外匯準備投資組合，顯示各國
央行對於增持黃金儲備的長期方向仍未改變 。世界黃金協會也預估 ，預期全球央行在 2026 年將會增
持約 850 噸黃金，增持水準將會與去年相近，顯示未來世界央行購金買盤仍可望為金價帶來支撐。
此外，展望後市 ，金價反轉的條件實際上也已在逐步成形，當前市場直覺認為高油價將導致通膨 ，
預期聯準會動作會偏鷹，導致金價表現受到壓抑。然而若戰事在短期內結束，預料市場將忽略短期
油價上漲的影響，使降息預期大致回升到戰前水準，有利金價表現；而若美伊戰爭 進一步擴大，將
可能使油價被推升至壓抑經濟表現的價格，屆時經濟更可能面臨的問題反而不是通膨而是衰退， 聯

準會更可能降息以救經濟，隨利率走低亦有利金價反彈。故 目前來看，隨金價回檔已深，重回多頭
條件也已在逐步成形，建議短線可持續觀望戰事變化，中性看待黃金類股後市表現。

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
固定收益：通膨疑慮雖推升利率，未來仍有降息可能
FOMC 3/18 會議釋出訊號略偏鷹派。投票結果 11 比 1，僅 Miran 主張降息一碼，而原先偏鴿
的 Waller 轉為支持按兵不動，內部對降息的共識正在弱化。會後聲明雖新增中東局勢不確定性的描
述，釋出政策需保持耐心的訊號，但整體對經濟評估仍維持「穩健」 ，未因2 月就業數據轉弱而顯著
調整措辭。點陣圖中位數雖未變動，但分布結構出現轉變。首先，預期降息的官員人數減少，支持多
次降息的比例下滑；其次，已出現個別官員預期未來升息的情境；此外，長期利率中位數上修，顯示

中性利率評估提高。
雖然因中東能源問題導致 Fed Funds 隔夜利率交換市場所估算今年底前的利率變動預期已從戰
事爆發前的降息 2 碼大幅縮減，近期更出現不排除升息的臆測。然而，自美以伊衝突以來至 3/26(台
北時間)， 包括高盛在內的投資銀行所發布對Fed 利率(區間上緣)預期均認為 FOMC 未來兩年仍將傾
向降息。若此預期得以實現，代表市場目前已過度反映 Fed 的「鷹派情境」，之後殖利率可望有回
穩的空間。
再者，3 月油價上揚，投資人對於未來美國貨幣政策轉向的疑慮升溫，確實也導致了美國甚至是
全球的主要債券市場殖利率上揚。然而，就在市場聚焦於通膨的同時，包括 Pimco、摩根大通和
Columbia Threadneedle Investments 等投資機構 3/30 不約而同表達，投資人低估了美國對伊朗
戰爭導致經濟急劇放緩的風險的看法，並認為這場衝擊最終將引發債券殖利率再度回落 。高盛 3 月下
旬的報告提醒，比起升息，中東戰事更大的風險在於(接近)經濟衰退，這可能觸發 Fed 更大幅度的降

息。Pimco 首席投資官 Daniel Ivascyn 也表示「通常始於通膨衝擊的局面，會迅速演變為成長衝擊…
而我們正處於經濟大幅走軟的邊緣。」因此，未來債券殖利率不排除有向下的可能性存在。
隨著美國 11 月期中選舉逼近，川普在政治上將面臨更大的壓力，以美國財政體質及川普向來的
政策來看，之後美國政府勢必將盡力壓低美國公債的殖利率，有利使美債利率處於相對可控的範圍。
近期美國公債殖利率雖因市場的通膨疑慮而上揚 ， 但目前殖利率遠高於Fed 長期通膨目標(2%)，
美債的高息收對長線投資人具強大誘因 ， 因此投資人仍可以資產配置角度對於債券作適當之配置 。 此
外，包括 Pimco、摩根大通等投資機構也認為未來債券殖利率有向下的可能性存在。以目前的殖利
率看，美債仍有投資空間。考量短期殖利率受市場對 Fed 的利率政策預期變動影響較大，故在年期
與券種的選擇上可以 7~15 年的中長期美國公債或投資級債為主。

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國製造與服務業維持擴張，惟就業降溫，貿易逆差縮減 
經濟 
⚫ 7 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數升至 55.6，創 2022 年 5 月以來新
高，並連續第七個月維持擴張，顯示製造業景氣持續增溫。新訂單、生
產及就業同步改善，其中就業指數為 33 個月來首度重返擴張；惟供應
商交貨時間持續延長，原物料價格仍居高檔，後續仍須關注供應鏈與地
緣政治風險。 
⚫ 7 月美國 ISM 服務業採購經理人指數為 54.1，較上月微升 0.1 個百分
點，連續 25 個月維持擴張。商業活動與新訂單指數分別升至 59.1 及
57.2，顯示需求動能增強；惟雇傭指數降至 47.4，重返收縮區間。價格
指數升至 70.3，反映通膨壓力仍高；供應鏈持續改善，整體服務業景氣
維持穩健擴張。 
⚫ 7 月美國非農就業人數減少 2.3 萬人，由前月修正後增加 2.0 萬人轉為
負成長，失業率則由 4.2%降至 4.1%。雖醫療保健業持續增聘人力，但
地方政府教育、零售業及金融活動就業減少，加上勞動參與率與就業人
口比率同步下滑，且 5 月、6 月新增就業人數合計下修 10.3 萬人，反映
美國勞動市場動能降溫。 
⚫ 6 月美國職位空缺數下降 17.8 萬個至 735.9 萬個，職缺率微降至 4.4%；
聘僱與離職人數分別增至 534.8 萬人及 535.1 萬人，整體勞動市場流動
性大致持穩。職缺增加主要來自運輸倉儲及公用事業與聯邦政府。 
⚫ 6 月美國商品與服務貿易逆差縮減至 733 億美元 ，較上月減少5.6%，主
因進口降幅大於出口；其中商品貿易逆差縮減、服務貿易順差擴大。累
計 2026 年上半年貿易逆差較去年同期大減 33.8%， 除出口持續成長外 ，
亦因去年提前拉貨墊高比較基期，使進口增幅明顯趨緩所致。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 2026 年 7 月底美日罕見聯手進場干預匯市，帶動日圓止跌回升。美國
並透過賣出歐元、買進日圓方式介入市場，以避免釋出美元全面走弱訊
號。未來雙方不排除再次協調干預，並研議運用美債擔保融通機制籌措
資金，顯示美日對匯市穩定及金融風險外溢問題的高度關注。

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.8.10 
一、 美國—製造與服務業維持擴張，惟就業降溫，貿易逆差縮減 
2026 年 7 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI)為 55.6，較
上月的 53.3 上升 2.3 點，創 2022 年 5 月以來新高，並已連續第七個月維持在 50
以上擴張區間，顯示美國製造業景氣持續升溫；此外，ISM 指出，該數據亦反映
美國整體經濟已連續 21 個月維持擴張 。 細項觀察 ， 新訂單指數由56.0 升至 56.7，
連續第七個月擴張，顯示需求動能維持穩健；生產指數由52.2 大幅升至 58.5，創
2021 年 11 月以來新高，顯示產出活動明顯增強；就業指數由 49.7 升至 52.8，為
33 個月來首度重返擴張區間 ， 反映企業增聘意願回升 。 供應商交貨指數由57.4 升
至 58.9，交貨時間連續第八個月延長；庫存指數則由 51.4 微降至 51.2，擴張動能
略為放緩。此外，價格指數由73.0 降至 71.1，連續第三個月下滑，顯示原物料成
本上漲壓力有所緩和；新出口訂單指數則由 48.5 回升至 53.0，重返擴張區間，反
映海外需求改善。整體而言，美國製造業景氣擴張力道較前月明顯增強，需求、
生產、就業及出口同步改善，惟供應鏈交期延長、原物料價格仍處相對高檔，以
及地緣政治風險等因素，仍是後續景氣發展的重要觀察重點。 
2026 年 7 月美國 ISM 服務業採購經理人指數(Services PMI) 為 54.1，較上月
54.0 微幅上升 0.1 個百分點，已連續 25 個月維持在 50 以上擴張區間，且高於近
12 個月平均值 53.4，顯示美國服務業景氣持續擴張。從細項觀察，商業活動指數
由 55.4 升至 59.1，創 2024 年 5 月以來次高水準；新訂單指數由 55.1 升至 57.2，
連續 14 個月維持擴張 ， 反映企業營運及市場需求動能持續增強 。 勞動市場方面 ，
雇傭指數由 51.2 降至 47.4，再度跌回收縮區間，為 2026 年 3 月以來最低水準，
顯示企業聘僱態度仍偏審慎。價格方面，價格指數由67.7 升至 70.3，為近五個月
第四度站上 70 以上，且 12 個月平均值升至 2023 年 4 月以來高點，顯示成本壓
力及通膨風險仍然偏高。供應鏈方面，供應商交期指數由54.4 降至 52.8，雖連續
20 個月高於 50，顯示交貨時間仍偏長，但已連續第三個月改善；庫存指數則由
51.2 微升至 51.4，維持擴張。另新出口訂單與進口指數分別升至 52.0 及 51.8，顯
示外部需求及採購活動有所回溫 。 整體而言 ，7 月美國服務業景氣維持穩健擴張 ，
商業活動與新訂單成長加速，惟就業表現轉弱且價格壓力再度升溫，顯示勞動市

場與通膨風險仍為後續觀察重點。 
2026 年 7 月美國非農就業人數較上月減少 2.3 萬人 ， 低於上月修正後增加2.0
萬人的表現，顯示就業市場動能轉為小幅收縮；惟失業率由 4.2%降至 4.1%，失
業人數降至約 691.6 萬人，整體勞動市場尚未明顯惡化。就產業別觀察，醫療保
健業就業增加 2.2 萬人，雖延續成長，但低於過去 12 個月平均每月增加 3.6 萬人
的水準；相較之下，地方政府教育、零售業及金融活動分別減少5.0 萬人、1.9 萬
人及 1.4 萬人，成為主要拖累。勞動市場結構方面，勞動參與率降至 61.4%，就
業人口比率降至 58.9%， 且自今年1 月以來分別累計下降 0.7 及 0.5 個百分點 ， 顯
示勞動供給與就業情勢較年初轉弱。薪資與工時方面，民營部門平均每週工時維
持 34.3 小時，平均時薪升至 37.62 美元，月增 0.1%、年增 3.2%，薪資成長仍屬
溫和。整體而言，7 月美國就業市場呈現降溫跡象，加上 5 月及 6 月新增就業人
數合計下修 10.3 萬人 ， 反映先前就業成長力道可能遭高估 ； 後續仍須關注服務業 、
政府部門及企業招聘動能是否進一步放緩。 
2026 年 6 月美國職位空缺數為 735.9 萬個，較上月減少 17.8 萬個，職缺率由
4.5%微降至 4.4%；聘僱人數為 534.8 萬人，較上月增加 9.6 萬人，聘僱率略升至
3.4%；總離職人數為 535.1 萬人，較上月增加 9.1 萬人，離職率亦升至 3.4%，顯
示勞動市場流動性大致持穩。其中，主動離職人數為323.2 萬人，較上月增加 7.9
萬人 ， 但離職率維持2.0%； 裁員與解僱人數為176.6 萬人 ， 較上月小增0.5 萬人 ，
裁員率維持 1.1%， 顯示勞工轉職意願及企業人力調整仍相對溫和 。 從產業別觀察 ，
職位空缺增加主要集中於運輸、倉儲及公用事業(增加 9.7 萬個)及聯邦政府(增加
3.9 萬個)，而批發業(減少 7.4 萬個)、非耐久財製造業(減少 5.5 萬個)及採礦與伐
木業(減少 0.9 萬個)則呈現下降。整體而言，6 月美國勞動市場呈現職缺、聘僱及
離職規模大致持穩的態勢，顯示勞動需求雖較疫情後高峰有所降溫，但仍維持一
定韌性，就業市場尚未出現明顯轉弱跡象。 
2026 年 6 月美國商品與服務貿易逆差縮減至 733 億美元，較上月減少 44 億
美元(-5.6%)； 當月出口降至3,147 億美元 ， 月減29 億美元(-0.9%)， 進口降至3,880
億美元，月減73 億美元(-1.8%)，由於進口降幅大於出口，帶動整體逆差縮小。結
構方面，商品貿易逆差由 1,060 億美元縮減至 1,021 億美元(-3.6%)，主要反映商
品進口回落，其中資本財及消費財降幅較大；服務貿易順差則由 283 億美元增至
288 億美元(+1.9%)，主要受金融服務及旅遊出口成長帶動。累計 2026 年 1 至 6

7

月，美國商品與服務貿易逆差為 3,712 億美元，較去年同期縮減 1,893 億美元(-
33.8%)，主因出口年增 11.7%，明顯高於進口的 0.4%。整體而言，6 月逆差縮減
主要反映商品進口降溫及服務順差擴大，顯示美國對外貿易失衡情況較前月有所
改善。 
2026 年 7 月底日圓兌美元一度貶至接近 164.0 日圓 、 創近40 年新低後 ， 美國
與日本罕見聯手進場干預匯市 ， 為1998 年亞洲金融危機以來首次協同買進日圓 ，
促使日圓止跌回升。此次美國並未循傳統方式賣出美元買進日圓，而是透過賣出
歐元、買進日圓介入市場，以避免對外釋出推動美元全面走弱的訊號。美國財長
貝森特並表示，不排除再次與日本協調干預，且正考慮擴大 Fed 的 FIMA 融通機
制，允許日本以持有的美債作為擔保籌措干預資金，避免因出售美債支撐日圓而
推升美債殖利率。整體而言，此次美日聯手干預除反映雙方對日圓過度貶值及匯
市波動的憂慮外，亦凸顯其對債市穩定與金融風險外溢的關注，並顯示美國外匯
政策出現較過去更積極介入市場的傾向。 
美國總統川普於 8 月 6 日宣布，依《1962 年貿易擴張法》第 232 條款，將自
2026 年 12 月 4 日起對多晶矽及其衍生產品課徵 15%關稅，並同步實施最低進口
價格制度，以防止中國低價傾銷並促進供應鏈回流美國。適用產品涵蓋多晶矽、
矽晶圓、太陽能電池及太陽能模組，其中多晶矽最低進口價格訂為每公斤 21 美
元。美方表示，多晶矽不僅是太陽能產業關鍵原料，也是半導體供應鏈的重要基
礎材料，此措施有助提升美國能源與晶片供應鏈安全。市場預期，中國太陽能產
業將首當其衝，美國短期內可能面臨太陽能產品價格上揚。由於台灣、日本、韓
國及歐盟產品適用較有利稅制，加上美國本土產能仍不足，台灣業者所受影響相
對有限，並有機會受惠於供應鏈重組及赴美投資商機。 
美國共和黨主導的參議院於 8 月 8 日以 90 票贊成 、6 票反對通過短期政府撥
款法案(Continuing Resolution) ，擬將聯邦政府現行資金延長至 12 月 11 日，以避
免現行預算於 9 月 30 日到期後導致聯邦政府停擺，並降低 11 月期中選舉前的政
治不確定性。由於共和黨控制的眾議院已於 7 月通過不同版本的短期撥款法案，
參眾兩院仍須進一步協商整合內容，才能送交川普總統簽署生效。參院版本除納
入住宅及糧食援助等相關經費調整機制外，亦限制行政部門將其他預算項目資金
轉用於邊境安全用途，並暫時凍結白宮管理及預算局(OMB)一項擴大政治任命官
員對聯邦補助資金審核權限的新規。整體而言，參院通過短期撥款法案有助於降

二、歐元區—第二季消費動能穩健，能否延續至第三季仍有不確定性 
 6 月零售銷售量月減 0.3%，但受到前兩個月強勁的成長支持，第二季零售額
仍季增 0.2%。另方面，5 月服務業生產指數月增 0.8%，其 4、5 月平均數較第一
季成長 1%，餐飲、住宿、不動產仲介服務為表現較佳者，預期6-7 月的世足賽熱
潮可望進一步增強娛樂及餐飲等需求成長。整體而言，雖然歐元區第二季通膨壓
力增強 ， 所幸在充足的家庭儲蓄以及穩健的就業市場支持下 ， 消費需求依然強勁 ，
惟 7 月起中東局勢升溫及能源價格上漲仍是後續需關注的下行風險。 
美國自 12 月起對歐盟多晶矽產品課徵 15%不疊加 MFN 關稅，惟 2025 年高
純度矽(HSCODE:280461，包含多晶矽、單晶矽棒等)出口至美國占總出口額僅約
0.002%，影響有限。 
三、日本—薪資持續成長，企業加薪動能延續 
6 月薪資收入年增 3.4%， 其中受惠於夏季獎金發放，帶動特別獎金年增 3.5%；
經常性薪資年增 3.4%；加班費年增 2.8%，顯示企業加薪趨勢持續擴大。另一方
面，政府計劃從 10 月起將最低工資調升約 4.9%，雖低於去年的增幅，但仍有助
於帶動中小企業提高薪資水準。整體而言，在薪資持續成長、最低工資調升、通
膨預期升溫及勞動力短缺等因素支撐下，薪資上漲動能可望延續，並為明年春鬥
談判加薪幅度形成支撐。 
四、中國—通膨放緩；出口動能強勁，但美國打壓中國出口管道增添未來變數 
7 月出口年增率較 6 月降 3.1 個百分點至 23.9%，出口總值為 3,979 億美元；
累計前七個月出口年增率為 18.5%，累積出口總值為 25,226 億美元。前七個月
IC、自動數據處理設備(包含伺服器等)累積出口額分別成長 99.5%、45.2%；船舶
累積出口成長 37.9%；汽車累積出口成長 54.9%。另方面，7 月進口年增率較 6 月
降 8.5 個百分點至 27.5%，進口總值為 2,854 億美元；累計前七個月進口值成長
11.6%至 13,069 億美元，其中 IC、自動數據處理設備(包含伺服器等)進口分別成
長 58.3%、93.2%，呼應中國對高階 IC 的依賴，以及部分伺服器關鍵組件有賴於
進口滿足所需。進出口相抵後，前七個月貿易順差達 6,874 億美元，略高於去年

展望未來 ， 由於美國國土安全部於新增43 家中國企業至 《維吾爾強迫勞動預

家，創下該法案實施以來的單次最大增列紀錄。本次清單涵蓋電容器、紡織、食
品等多個廠商，短期內將面臨訂單轉移及市場拓展壓力，進而限制出口動能。 
7 月生產者通膨(出廠價格)由 6 月的 4.1%降至 3.5%，儘管該月布蘭特原油價
格大幅上漲，但中國石油和天然氣開採業出廠價格年增率大降 13.6 個百分點至
3.2%，其餘石化相關產業如化學原料、燃料加工業出廠價格年增率亦有收斂，價
格對國際原油波動存在反應滯後的特性。另因金價盤整，有色金屬礦採選業出廠
價格年增率亦持續放慢。 
7 月消費者通膨(CPI)降 0.5 個百分點為 0.5%，核心通膨(不含食品及能源)略
降 0.1 個百分點至 0.9 %。該月燃油通膨降 14.5 個百分點至 0.8%為主要因素，並
抑制旅遊服務價格漲幅，其他商品及服務價格則大致平穩。整體而言，7 月生產
者與消費者通膨同步放緩，反映能源及上游原物料價格漲勢趨緩，惟國際原油價
格近期上漲，考量能源成本傳導存在時間差，未來通膨仍有回升風險。 
五、印度及東南亞—東協製造業景氣保持強勁擴張，印度央行維持利率不變 
印度: 印度央行（RBI）8 月會議維持基準利率(Repo Rate)於 5.25%不變，保持中
性立場。經濟及通膨展望方面，上調 2026/27 財年的 GDP 成長預期 0.1 個百分點
至 6.7%，預估 2026/27 財年平均及核心通膨率(CPI)為 5.0%、4.3%，央行認為近
期通膨上升主要受食品與燃料等供給面因素影響，尚未形成廣泛性物價上漲。在
經濟成長仍具韌性，但季風、油價、地緣政治及全球貿易政策存在高度不確定性
情況下，RBI 將靜待通膨走勢更為明朗後再決定是否調整利率。 
東南亞: 
(1)7 月 S&P Global 東協(ASEAN)製造業採購經理人指數(PMI)由 6 月的 50.5 升至

2023 年 4 月以來最高。按個別國家來看，7 月除印尼(50.2)自收縮轉為擴張，馬來

西亞持平上月 50.7，新加坡(59.2)、泰國(54.2)、菲律賓(51.8)、越南(52.9)均加速
擴張。整體而言，儘管AI 基礎建設熱潮、個別國家振興消費政策，持續帶動新訂
單及生產活動，東協國家製造業持續保持擴張，惟中東衝突的不確定性仍存，部
分國家緊縮貨幣，仍須留意生產、借貸成本同步上揚下，可能壓縮廠商獲利。 
(2)印尼：2026 年第 2 季經濟成長率(GDP)年增 5.29%，低於前季的 5.61%，其中，
民間消費成長 5.06%為最大成長來源，主要受開齋節後續消費、旅遊餐飲及交通
支出增加帶動；儘管財政預算支出大幅下降，政府支出仍成長15.97%；固定資本
形成（投資）成長 6.87%，主要由公共基礎設施所帶動；惟進口成長率(8.82%)遠
高於出口成長率(4.13%)， 淨出口拖累0.78 個百分點。 以產業面觀之 ， 製造業成長
4.52%為成長主力 ， 惟礦業受出口產量限制影響表現較弱 。 整體而言 ， 能源價格衝
擊和貨幣政策收緊持續壓抑家庭消費，並拖累淨出口表現，在財政空間收窄的情
況下，下半年政府支出對經濟貢獻將有所收斂。 
(3)菲律賓:2026 年第 2 季 GDP 年增率降至 2.3%，低於第 1 季的 2.8%，創疫情以
來新低，其中，民間消費年增2.8%增速偏緩，反映高通膨及能源價格上漲侵蝕購
買力，政府消費成長 8.3%為支撐經濟主要力量；固定資本形成大幅衰退 13.7%，
反映公共建設支出縮減及民間投資趨於保守，成為拖累經濟成長的主因；另受惠
電子產品及 AI 相關需求，出口年增 12.2%，進口放緩至 5.5%，淨出口對 GDP 積
極貢獻。從產業面觀察，服務業年增4.5%，仍是主要成長引擎，其中批發及零售
業成長 4.6%、 教育服務成長12.7%表現突出 ； 製造業僅成長2.6%， 主要反映公共
建設投資減少導致營建活動疲弱，致使工業部門衰退 2.4%所致；農林漁業成長
2.7%。整體而言，菲律賓經濟仍受投資不振與物價壓力制約，未來成長有賴政府
加速公共支出執行及投資信心回升。 
六、台灣—通膨壓力升溫，出口動能續強 
7 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 2.54%，雖較 6 月略為回落，但已連續 3 個月
高於 2%通膨警戒線，主要受颱風豪雨推升蔬果價格，以及暑期旅遊旺季帶動團
費與機票上漲所致；此外，外食費年增 3.05%、房租年增 1.73%，使服務類價格
持續上升。另一方面，7 月生產者物價指數(PPI)年增 16.94%，創 4 年半以來最大
漲幅；以新臺幣計價之進口及出口物價指數則分別年增 28.19%及 31.56%，均創
近 50 年來最大漲幅，主要受原油及 AI 相關電子零組件價格上揚帶動，惟電子零

組件進口多供出口生產使用，加上政府透過油價平穩機制減緩能源價格衝擊，因
此目前尚未出現明顯輸入性通膨 。 整體而言 ， 外食 、 房租及旅遊等價格持續上漲 ，
使服務類通膨維持較高漲幅，主計總處預估 8 月通膨仍將維持在 2%之上。 
7 月出口達 753 億美元 ， 年增32.9%，主要係全球經濟展現高於預期的韌性 、
AI 基礎建設及高效能運算需求持續擴張 、 國際品牌科技新品進入備貨期 ， 以及電
子產業供應鏈偏緊帶動價格上揚等因素，支撐出口成長動能。隨著電子產業旺季
來臨，加上台積電等指標企業持續上修營收與資本支出展望，顯示企業對 AI 相
關需求前景仍抱持樂觀看法，財政部預期下半年出口表現可望維持強勁，全年出
口金額有望突破 8,500 億美元，再創歷史新高。惟目前出口成長仍高度仰賴美國
AI 投資需求，未來仍須關注美國景氣變化、AI 投資動能及貿易政策調整可能帶
來的風險。 
美國總統川普於美東時間 8 月 6 日簽署總統公告 ， 依據 《1962 年貿易擴張法》
第 232 條款調查結果，宣布自 2026 年 12 月 4 日起對多晶矽及其衍生產品課徵
15%關稅，並建立最低進口價格（Minimum Import Price, MIP）機制，以保護美國
半導體及太陽能產業供應鏈，降低對海外低價產能的依賴。其中，臺灣、日本、
韓國及歐盟適用 15%不疊加 MFN 關稅之稅率，較其他國家具相對有利條件。行
政院臺美經貿工作小組表示，我國多晶矽相關產品出口美國約 3.2 億美元，其中
以半導體用矽晶圓為主，出口金額約 2.5 億美元。由於我國半導體業者近年已積
極赴美投資，可依臺美投資合作 MOU 享有關稅豁免配額，以及建廠與營運所需
原料、設備及零組件之關稅優惠，因此預估本次措施對國內半導體產業衝擊相對
有限。至於太陽光電產業，由於對美出口僅占整體產值約 1%，且以內需市場為
主，政府評估受影響程度不大。

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

1 
3605 宏致(ACES)

報告日期： 2026/08/06  研 究 員：洪瑞陽  (Ryan Hung) 
壹、投 資 建 議

一、 2Q26 營收 33.5 億元，預估稅後 EPS 1.27 元 
2Q26 合併營收  33.5 億元， QoQ +18.8% 、YoY +20.2% ，創同期
歷史新高。三率同步改善 ： 毛利率 26.3% （QoQ +1.7ppts 、YoY 約
持平） 、營業利益率 7.65% （QoQ +2.4ppts ） 、稅後淨利率 6.2% ；
單季稅後淨利  2.08 億元 、EPS 1.27 元 ， 累計上半年營收 61.7 億
元（ YoY +15% ）、 EPS 1.93 元。改善動能來自  AI 高毛利產品
放量與稼動率回升，預估 AI（高速連接器＋高速傳輸線＋ Bloom 
Energy 電源線）營收占比由  1Q 的 14% 躍升至  2Q 的 20% 。

二、 AI 相關廠品高速連接器與電源線，切入美系大廠  
成長由高速連接器、高速傳輸線、 Bloom Energy （BE）大功率電
源線三大產品線構成，預估 2Q26 合計已占營收  20% ，為獲利結
構改善的核心。主力高速連接器最強：產品由過去硬碟、風扇、
記憶體等功能性小卡，逐步打入伺服器主機板與  CPU ／GPU ，並
已切入  CSP 大廠  AWS 、Meta 的 ASIC 伺服器。高速傳輸線：
已通過兩家  CSP 認證並於今年出貨 ； 惟因高度自製 、 每案規格不
同，逐季墊高，現階段聚焦機內線。 BE 大功率電源線 2H26 起放
量，隨其電廠模組化程度提升與新案推進，今年切入新案供應、
需求逐季提升並已規劃擴產；此塊沿用既有製造與工程能力、新
增資本與研發投入少，且毛利水準佳，隨  BE 新案放量，營收與
獲利貢獻將逐步提升。

三、預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 5.51/7.55 元 
AI 產品組合拉升，預估  2026 年 11.79 億元（ YoY +97.5% ）、
2027 年 16.37 億元（YoY +38.8% ）； 營業利益率同步由 2024 年 
2.9% 攀升至  2027 年  10.2% 。預估  2026 年營收  138.3 億元
（YoY +27.3% ）、 毛 利 率 26.4% 、稅後淨利  9.63 億元、EPS 5.51 
元； 2027 年營收  160.6 億元（ YoY +16.1% ）、毛利率  27.6% 、
稅後淨利  13.18 億元、 EPS 7.55 元。

四、投資建議：買進，目標價 136 元 
由傳統消費性連接器，轉向  AI 高速連接器、高速傳輸線與大功
率電源線，產品組合結構性升級，以過去正常獲利水準本益比約
在 12-18 倍 之 間，而 現 在 有AI 新題材成長 ， 屬於新一塊獲利貢獻，
給予 18 倍本益比，以 2027 EPS 計算，目標價 136 元，建議買進。

貳、公 司 概 況 
一、公司簡介  
宏致成立於  1996 年，總部位於台灣桃園，專注於高階精密連接
器與連接線的研發與製造，超過  5,000 名員工，橫跨亞洲、美洲
與歐洲建構完整製造與服務網絡。從設計、模具、沖壓、電鍍，
到組裝與測試的全方位製造服務，致力於成為全球客戶最信賴的
連接解決方案夥伴。全球擁有 6 大製造地區、 10 座生產工廠，遍
佈 11 國的全球業務服務據點 。 三大事業群 ： 連接器事業群 （ACES 
宏致）：專注於高速、高頻、高可靠性連接器的設計與製造，具備
模具 、 沖壓 、 電鍍及整合組裝能力 。 連接線事業群 （MEC 龍翰  ）：
專注於高速傳輸線材、模組與車用線束製造，支援多樣化應用需
求。金屬沖壓事業群（ KYN 廣迎新）：專注於全方位金屬結構件
製造，具備  NCT 數控沖孔加工機、雷射切割與折彎設備，涵蓋
各式金屬支架、電磁屏蔽罩、伺服器機殼、機箱機櫃等精密結構
件加工，並可延伸至  Level 3 機構組裝階段，實現整合方案一站
式供應。公司長期以來的併購形成不同應用別的完整布局，目前
連接器與連接線相關可分成五大產品應別：工業 (不斷電系統、智
能工業設備 、 工業電腦)、 一般網通相關(通用型伺服器 、 網路設備、
機上盒 )、AI 相關 (資料中心、高速連接 )、車用電子、電腦相關消
費性電子。

資料來源：公司資料、自行預估

二、 AI 高速連接相關產品放量，帶動獲利成長  
宏致高速連接相關產品主要分為高速連接器與高速連接線。  
高速連接器由過去應用於硬碟、風扇、記憶體等功能性小卡，逐
步打入伺服器主機板及  CPU/GPU 相關應用，並已切入 CSP 大廠
AWS 和 Meta 的 ASIC 伺服器。 2025 年以前僅出小板； 2026 年隨
CSP 主導晶片設計，除了小板也開始供應主板， ASIC 以整機的量
去看待，主要產品為 MCIO ，有 20 幾種規格  宏致全規格出貨。
預估單機櫃高速連接器用量由過去  8~10 顆倍增至  20 顆以上；
ASIC 機種因宏致同時供應主板與小板，單櫃可達  30 顆。月產
能由去年約  200 萬顆逐步提升至今年底上看  500 萬 顆，提 升2.5
倍，可見需求大增，產能滿載，開始放量。  
高速傳輸線，宏致從裸線開始自製、規格自開，與市場多向外部
採購裸線再組裝的模式差異化。產品經  2024~2025 逾一年驗證，
已通過兩家  CSP 認證，今年開始出貨。由於高速傳輸線高度自製
化，每一家規格都不同，放量較連接器和緩， 1H26 僅占營收約  1
％，逐季墊高，不像高速連接器比重提升快速。另外，宏致產品
主要是機內線，外部機櫃間傳輸線與解決方案目前還在努力中。

三、高壓電源線獲 Bloom Energy 訂單， 2H26 開始放量  
Bloom Energy （BE）近年為加速電廠建置，逐步採用模組化電源
系統，透過標準化電源模組進行彈性組裝，可依實際電力需求調
整系統規模。整體架構可大致分為供電與配電兩大部分，其中供
電端涉及發電及高功率電力轉換，配電端則負責後續電力分配。
由於各電源模組之間需要高壓線束進行電力傳輸與連接，此外電
力轉換設備內部亦有線束需求，因此  BE 電廠模組化程度提升，
將同步帶動高壓、高功率線束的使用量。  
宏致過去即為  BE 菲律賓廠既有供應商，惟過往相關業務規模有
限。隨  BE 近年營運狀況改善，加上新電廠方案逐步推進，宏致
自今年開始切入新案供應，並預期後續需求將逐季提升。公司目
前已針對  BE 需求規劃擴產，顯示其對該客戶後續需求能見度已
有一定掌握。

BE 的供應鏈並非由單一廠商獨占，而是採取分散供應策略；目前
宏致則透過高功率線束切入供電端。由於高壓線束可直接應用於
電源模組間的電力傳輸， 以及電力轉換設備內部連接， BE 電廠建
置規模擴大將有望帶動相關線束需求同步成長。相較高速傳輸線
束需要投入較多研發資源，高功率線束主要沿用公司既有製造與
工程能力，因此新增資本及研發投入相對少。公司亦表示相關產
品具備不錯的毛利水準，若後續  BE 新案持續放量，營收與獲利
的貢獻將逐步提升。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估 
一、預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 為 5.51 元， YOY+36.4%  
以公司  2Q26 營收為基礎逐季推估，考量  AI 高速連接器月產能
由約  200 萬顆倍增至年底  500 萬顆、稼動率自  1Q 低點回升並
趨於滿載，以及  AI 高階產品組合占比由  2Q26 的 20% 擴大至  
4Q 的  30% ，預估  3Q26 ／ 4Q26 營收分別為  36.66 ／ 39.88 億
元，全年營收  138.26 億元、 YoY +27.3% 。獲利部分，毛利率由  
2Q26 的 26.3% 逐季走高至  4Q 的 27.2% 、全年  26.4% ；營業
利益率全年  8.5% ； 全年稅後純益 9.63 億 元、稅 後 EPS 5.51 元。
營業利益（ YoY +97.5% ）與稅後純益（ YoY +45.7% ）成長幅度均
顯著高於營收，反映高毛利  AI 產品組合擴大的高獲利  
二、預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 為 7.55 元， YOY+36.9% 
展望  2027 年，隨  AI 伺服器需求延續帶動高速連接器供貨份額
提升、高速傳輸線與  Bloom Energy 電源線放量，以及高階產品
組合占比續揚，預估全年營收  160.55 億元、 YoY +16.1% 。獲利
結構進一步優化，毛利率升至  27.6% 、營業利益率升至  10.1% 。
全年稅後純益  13.18 億元、稅後  EPS 7.55 元。

Company Brief 
자동차/모빌리티 
Analyst 김준성 
joonsung.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 배현서 
hyeonseo.bae@meritz.co.kr 2Q26 방어력 입증  3Q26 공격력 공개  
2Q26 실적, 비교우위 방어력 입증 
매출 33.0조원으로 컨센서스를 +5% 상회했다. 지배주주 순이익은 2.33조원으로 컨
센서스에 부합했다. 스마트카와 저가 전기차 공세 속에서 현대차를 포함한 레거시 
OEM 중 비교우위의 판매 및 실적 방어력을 입증한 분기 실적이었다. 
- 2Q26 YoY 전세계 도매 판매 성장률: 현대차 -7% vs. 기아 +5%. 
- 2Q26 YoY 매출 성장률: 현대차 차량 부문 +2% vs. 기아 +13%. 
- 2Q26 YoY 영업이익 성장률: 현대차 차량 부문 -27% vs. 기아 -5%. 
현대와 기아 모두 우호적 환율 효과를 통해 판매 성과 이상의 매출 실적을 기록했다. 
그러나 경쟁 심화에 따른 인센티브 지급액 (할인) 확대가 수익성 훼손 (매출 성장보다 
낮은 영업이익 성장률)을 야기 중이다. 다만, 현대와 기아의 방어력 차이는 확연하다. 
이를 보여주는 가장 뚜렷한 비교 지표가 BEV를 포함한 xEV 판매 성과다.  
- 2Q26 전세계 BEV 판매 성과: 현대차 7만대 (-13%) vs. 기아 11만대 (+88%). 
- 2Q26 전세계 xEV 판매 성과: 현대차 27만대 (+2%) vs. 기아 30만대 (+60%).

3Q26, 새로운 시장으로의 공격력을 입증할 시간 
가격 경쟁력이 뛰어난 BEV 라인업 (EV2~5) 출시를 통해 생산 가동률부터 영업 
현금흐름까지 단단한 흐름을 이어가고 있는 방어력은 입증됐다. 이제 스마트카와 
휴머노이드 등 피지컬 AI 시장을 향한 공격력을 입증할 시간이다. 
피지컬 AI가 태동하기 시작한 2020년 이후 기아 주가는 두 가지 특징을 보여왔
다. 1) 기존 사업 (차량 제조 및 판매) 실적의 주가 설명력이 상실했다. 2004 -
2020년 분기 순이익과 분기말 시가총액의 상관계수는 0.83이었으나, 2020년 이
후 현재까지의 상관계수는 0.59에 불과하다. 현재 주가는 미래에 벌어들인 총 현
금흐름의 할인 값이다. 기존 사업의 단기 성과가 미래 가치를 설명하지 못하는 
상황이다. 2) 이에 따라 주가는 새로운 사업에 대한 기대치가 높아질 때 반등했
다. 2021 년초 Apple 협력, 2025 년말 Nvidia 협력, 2026 년초 Google 협력 국
면의 주가 상승이 대표적 사례이다.  
3Q26 현대차그룹은 새만금 SPC (AI 데이터센터 및 로봇 제조) 구축, RMAC (휴
머노이드 트레이닝 센터) 가동, Robotics America ( 미국 로봇 제조) 설립 등 새
로운 사업을 위한 구체적 준비 현황과 방향을 공개한다. 구체적 역할 확립이 이
루어질 기아에 대해 기존 Buy 투자의견과 220,000원 적정주가를 유지한다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 7. 27

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 220,000원 
현재주가 (7.24) 130,500원 
상승여력 68.6%

### Page 1

2026.07.24   
삼성바이오로직스  (207940)

성장을 이끄는 새로운 스토리가 생겼다 Company Brief

무난한 2분기 실적, 다음 분기부터는 5공장과 미국 공장도 매출 발생 전망 
동사는 2분기 매출액 1조 3,209억원(YoY +30%), 영업이익 5,864억원(YoY +23%, 
OPM 44%) 을 달성했다. 컨센서스(매출 1조 3,222억원, 영업이익 5,997억원) 대비 
이익이 소폭 하회했다. 미국 락빌 생산시설 및 5공장 관련 매출 인식 전 비용이 
선반영된 효과에 기인한다. 1~4공장은 2분기에도 풀 가동 체제가 그대로 유지됐다. 
3분기부터는 5공장 및 미국 공장에서 매출이 발생할 것이 기대된다. 또한 3분기 중 
네덜란드에 세일즈오피스를 설립해 미국, 아시아에 이은 글로벌 3대 핵심 시장의 영업 
거점을 모두 확보하게 된다. 주요 지역별 고객 수요에 신속한 대응이 가능해지며 이를 
통해 현지 고객사와의 커뮤니케이션을 확대함으로써 신규 수주까지 이어질 수 있다. 
최근 노이즈가 있던 노조 파업 이슈와 관련한 매출 영향은 3분기에 발생할 것으로 
판단된다. 그러나 예정된 납품 일정에는 큰 차질없이 제품 공급이 가능하고, 고환율 
환경에 따른 동사의 수혜를 고려 시 연간 가이던스(전년대비 매출 15~20% 성장)에 
미치는 영향은 제한적일 것으로 보인다.

폴리펩타이드 그룹 인수를 통해 확인한 글로벌로 나아가는 공격적인 행보 
동사는 7/20일 스위스 소재 펩타이드 전문 CDMO 기업인 폴리펩타이드 그룹을 
인수한다는 공시를 발표했다. 인수금액은 국내 최대 규모인 2.7조원이며 폴리펩타이드 
그룹의 발행 주식 100%를 공개매수하는 전액 현금거래 방식이다. 
주당 매수가액은 44.31프랑이며 공시 전 거래일 종가 기준 약 6% 프리미엄을 부여한 
수치다. 최대주주는 55.65% 지분을 보유한 드라우프니프 홀딩(Draupnir Holding 
B.V.)이며 공개매수 참여를 확약했다. 유통물량 중 약 10% 남짓을 확보하면 
목표수준에 도달한다는 점에서 거래의 불확실성은 해소되었다고 판단한다. 올해말까지 
규제 승인을 포함한 모든 절차를 마무리하고 인수 완료 시 폴리펩타이드 그룹은 
스위스증권거래소(SIX)에서 상장 폐지되고 동사의 완전 자회사로 편입된다. 
폴리펩타이드 그룹은 1996년 페링 파마슈티컬스의 펩타이드 생산 사업부에서 
분사하여 설립됐다. 약 70년에 달하는 펩타이드 API 제조 경력을 보유하고 있다. 현재 
글로벌에서 1,000건 이상의 트랙 레코드를 확보했으며 펩타이드 관련 글로벌 임상 
3상의 30% 이상에서 핵심 제조 파트너로 참여 중이다. 폴리펩타이드 그룹은 스위스 
본사를 포함해 글로벌 5개국에서 생산 거점을 보유했다. 이는 미국, 유럽, 아시아를 
아우르는 배치로, 최근 사이트 분산에 대한 필요성이 커지는 업계 트렌드에 부합한다.

목표주가 190만원으로 하향. 다변화되는 CDMO 포트폴리오는 긍정적 
동사에 대한 투자의견은 그대로 유지하되 목표주가를 190만원으로 하향한다. DCF 
밸류에이션을 통해 산출된 수치이며 Terminal growth는 CDMO 산업 자체의 성장성이 
견고하고 미-중 갈등에 따른 반사이익을 볼 수 있는 가능성을 고려해 4%로 설정했다. 
또한 실적 추정치 및 이번 실적발표를 통해 6공장 착공 및 제3캠퍼스 관련 업데이트를 
진행한 점을 반영해 시설 관련 투자 수치를 조정했다. 
항체의약품 중심의 영역에서 ADC, CGT 등 신규 모달리티에 대한 생산시설 확보를 
추구 중이며 금번 인수를 통해 펩타이드 영역까지 사업 영역을 확장하고 있다. 시장이 
걱정했던 수주 관련 진행상황에 대해 다수의 고객사들과 논의 중임을 실적발표 세션을 
통해 언급하였기에 큰 우려는 없다는 판단이다.

Compliance notice 
당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

[투자의견] 
종목추천 투자등급  
종목투자의견은 향후 12개월간 추천일 종가대비 해당종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함.  
 Buy(매수): 추천일 종가대비 +15% 이상  
 Hold(보유): 추천일 종가대비 -15% ~ 15% 내외 등락 
 Sell(매도): 추천일 종가대비 -15% 이상 
산업추천 투자등급

 Overweight(비중확대) 
 Neutral(중립) 
 Underweight(비중축소)

### Page 2

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX có xu hướng giảm trong tuần qua, -40.89 điểm tương ứng -2.24%, với thanh 
khoản đi ngang. Hàng xa xỉ & dệt, may, Dịch vụ phân phối, Vật liệu xây dựng là các nhóm 
ngành có mức giảm rõ nét nhất trong tuần. Khối ngoại có động thái bán ròng với giá trị 
2,134 tỷ đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, CTG và VPB; đồng thời, tự doanh bán ròng ở mức 
129 tỷ đồng. 
Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại mua ròng 2,040 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua và đang 
duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế 647 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 3,600 hợp đồng và đang duy trì 
vị thế mua lũy kế 1,564 hợp đồng. 
TTCK Việt Nam có tuần điều chỉnh thứ 3 liên tiếp khi VN-Index giảm xuống dưới ngưỡng 
1.800 điểm, thanh khoản ở mức thấp và khối ngoại tiếp tục bán ròng. Áp lực bán xuất hiện 
ngay từ phiên đầu tuần sau thông tin căng thẳng Mỹ - Iran bùng phát trở lại, khiến giá dầu 
bật tăng mạnh và đảo chiều các kỳ vọng về thỏa thuận hòa bình trước đó. Bên cạnh đó, 
việc mặt bằng lãi suất duy trì ở mức cao, và thông tin tiêu cực về doanh nghiệp đầu ngành 
bán lẻ trang sức PNJ bị điều tra cũng gây áp lực lên tâm lý nhà đầu tư. Mùa báo cáo KQKD 
quý 2 đang diễn ra với nhiều điểm sáng về tăng trưởng, tuy nhiên không đủ nâng đỡ thị 
trường chung.

17/07/2026

Triển vọng kinh tế 2H2026 
Chủ động thích ứng, củng cố tăng trưởng

Một số dự báo chính của KBSV về kinh tế Việt Nam trong năm 2026:

1) Chúng tôi dự báo tăng trưởng GDP năm 2026 đạt 8.0 - 8.5%, với động lực 
tăng trưởng chính đến từ kỳ vọng Chính phủ đẩy mạnh giải ngân đầu tư 
công, dòng vốn FDI đầu tư vào Việt Nam duy trì xu hướng tích cực và khu 
vực sản xuất công nghiệp tăng trưởng tốt theo đà phục hồi của hoạt động 
xuất nhập khẩu. Về rủi ro, chúng tôi cho rằng 2H2026, Việt Nam sẽ tiếp tục 
đối mặt với một số yếu tố bất định từ bên ngoài, bao gồm xung đột Mỹ – 
Iran có thể kéo dài hơn dự kiến, nguy cơ vỡ bong bóng AI cùng những 
căng thẳng Trung Quốc – Đài Loan…

2) Chúng tôi hạ dự báo lạm phát bình quân năm 2026 của Việt Nam về 
quanh mức 4.5% YoY tương đương mục tiêu kiểm soát lạm phát của 
Chính phủ, dựa trên diễn biến khả quan của tình hình địa chính trị và giá 
năng lượng thế giới trong 2H2026.

3) Chúng tôi đánh giá áp lực tỷ giá USD/VND trong 2H2026 sẽ tương đối ổn 
định, với dự báo tăng 1.5 - 2% trong năm 2026 chủ yếu nhờ chênh lệch 
dương lãi suất VND-USD, nguồn cung ngoại tệ dồi dào từ nâng hạng thị 
trường và dòng FDI công nghệ cao.

4) Chúng tôi cho rằng lãi suất huy động đã đang ở vùng đỉnh, sẽ tiếp tục neo 
cao trong quý 3, và k ỳ vọng có thể giảm nhẹ vào đầu quý 4 nhờ áp lực tỷ 
giá - lạm phát giảm, các biện pháp hỗ trợ thanh khoản/hạ lãi suất từ NHNN, 
nhu cầu nắm giữ tiền mặt hạ nhiệt và nguồn tiền từ đầu tư công được giải 
phóng vào hệ thống.

Document 338: El Nino_ The next supply shock_ (2026-07-20)

Source PDF: all report pdf/El Nino_ The next supply shock_ (2026-07-20).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-07-20
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

1 
Authors 
Henry Allen 
Macro Strategist

July 20, 2026 
Current forecasts point to one of the strongest El Nino events on record in Q4. 
That matters because El Nino events can act as a multi-dimensional supply 
shock, with a causal link to higher food and energy prices, alongside broader 
supply-chain disruption. This would be problematic at any point in time, but it’s a 
particular issue today because of existing supply-chain stresses and the 
backdrop of above-target inflation. So a very strong El Nino event would be 
another negative supply shock, at a point when the global economy has limited 
room to absorb one. 
This paper explores what an El Nino is, and why a very strong one later this year 
would be so concerning. It also takes a look at what happened on previous 
occasions, and the channels through which it affects the broader economy and 
financial markets. 
We also consider the potential consequences this time round. Much will be 
weather dependent, but the precedents from the 1970s show how an El Nino 
event can coalesce with other shocks to keep inflation persistently above target, 
just as we’ve seen in recent years. Moreover, this is particularly damaging for 
many EM economies, where food can make up as much as a third of consumer 
expenditure.

El Nino: The next supply shock?

### Page 2

20 July 2026
Thematic Research
Page 2 Deutsche Bank AG
What is an El Nino?
n An El Nino event is the warm phase of the El Nino-Southern Oscillation
(ENSO), which is a variation in winds and sea surface temperatures in the
Pacific Ocean. This oscillates between warming phases (called El Nino) and
cooling phases (called La Nina).
n The warming El Nino phase occurs when there are unusually warm sea
surface temperatures in the eastern Pacific. These warmer waters mean

that the Pacific jet stream moves south relative to normal, which creates
changes in weather patterns and ecosystems.
n We can see the effect on global temperatures too. Even as temperatures
have ben rising anyway over recent decades, the record years have often
coincided with El Nino events, including 1998, 2016, and 2024.
n Unfortunately, El Nino events are correlated with a higher frequency of
natural disasters, with more people affected by disasters in El Nino years

relative to neutral years. Flooding is one that sees a particular increase, but
it is also associated with temperature changes too.
n The effects of an El Nino vary considerably by region. In the US, it normally
coincides with wetter conditions in the southern US along the Gulf Coast.
In stronger episodes, this can extend to California and the Southwest too.
But in other regions it is more likely to cause drought, like Australia, whilst
India sees weaker monsoon rains.

n In practical terms the main effect of an El Nino is disruption to food
production. But there are other effects too. El Ninos have been correlated
with the spread of climate-sensitive diseases, and one study found that the
El Nino in 2015 contributed to the spread of the Zika virus since it created
conditions beneficial for mosquito-borne transmission. 1  Furthermore,
natural disasters like flooding increase the frequency of poor hygiene
conditions or undernutrition, so there is an impact there too, and El Ninos

have been linked to many famines and even conflicts.
n For markets, an El Nino is capable of hitting agricultural production,
hydropower, shipping, and even has effects on public health and political
stability. In other words, it operates as a multi-channel supply shock.

1901-2000 average (degrees celsius)

What’s particularly bad about this one?
El Nino cycles typically repeat every few years with irregular frequency. However,
today’s episode is particularly concerning because forecasts suggest it will be a
very strong one, ranking among the most serious in recent history.

Among others, the US Climate Prediction Center have said this could be one of the
strongest in over 75 years, with an 81% chance of it reaching the “very strong”
threshold over Oct-Dec. It even has a 73% chance of reaching the “strong”
threshold of 1.5C as soon as the July-September period.

A very strong El Nino means it’s more likely to shift rainfall and temperature patterns
across multiple regions simultaneously. And from a macro perspective, the risks are
non-linear. For instance, a modest food shock can be absorbed via inventories,
imports or substitution. But a large shock propagating across multiple countries
can’t be absorbed as easily, with second-round inflation effects more likely to result.

The timing is particularly bad right now, because the global economy is already
facing significant supply-chain stress. Most notably, the closure of the Strait of
Hormuz has led to a spike in oil and gas prices this year. And even if the El Nino is
temporary, the resulting inflation shock will push inflation higher at a time when it’s
Figure 3: US Climate Prediction Center forecasts (%) for El Nino

### Page 4

20 July 2026
Thematic Research
Page 4 Deutsche Bank AG
already above target in many countries.
These risks are already being considered by policymakers in decision making,
particularly in emerging markets. Indeed, this year has seen central banks in India,
Peru and Colombia all reference El Nino in official publications.
What happened on previous occasions?
2023-24: Supply-chain disruption and record temperatures occurred with the last
El Nino.
n The most recent El Nino offered a clear example of what can happen, and

this didn’t even hit the “very strong” threshold.
n 2024 was the warmest year on record, and the episode saw severe
droughts in parts of the world, including parts of South America, along with
severe flooding in East Africa and South America, consistent with the
typical redistribution of rainfall during El Nino.
n Meanwhile, the Panama Canal had its 3rd driest year on record in 2023,
meaning that authorities had to cut shipping passing through. The

disruption to commercial shipping demonstrated how El Nino is more than
just a food price story.
2014-16: The last “very strong” El Nino had broader food security and public
health implications
n Just as in 2024, the El Nino of 2016 also saw the biggest upward global
temperature anomaly to date.
n Ethiopia suffered its worst drought in decades, leaving millions of people in
need of food assistance. Moreover, the Pacific hurricane seasons saw 16
hurricanes in each of 2014 and 2015, the joint-highest number of record.

n One study found that El Nino was a contributing factor to the spread of the
Zika virus, since it created conditions beneficial for mosquito-borne
transmission.
1997-98: The “very strong” El Nino also had major climactic and public health
implications
n 1998 was another record year for global temperatures, and included many
extremes around the world. For instance, San Francisco saw its wettest
rainfall season since 1889-90, whilst Peru saw 16 times its average rainfall.

n Meanwhile, after major flooding in east Africa, there was an outbreak of Rift
Valley fever in Kenya, Somalia and Tanzania. The outbreak also led to major
losses of livestock too.
1982-83: The El Nino occurs when high inflation is still lingering
n By 1982-83, inflation had fallen substantially from its earlier heights, aided
by monetary tightening like that seen under Paul Volcker at the Fed.
However, the El Nino didn’t help matters, as it caused major climactic

disruption once again, including flooding in the United States , and
droughts in Indonesia and Australia.
1972-73: The El Nino played a role in raising inflation before the first oil shock

### Page 5

20 July 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 5
n Although the oil shock of 1973 was the primary factor that drove the
stagflation of the 1970s, several other shocks including the El Nino had
already occurred beforehand. This was significant because collectively,
they served to push up inflation and inflation expectations. For instance, US
fiscal spending had already risen in the late-1960s, thanks to the Johnson
Administration’s Great Society programmes and the Vietnam War. Then in

1971, President Nixon ended the dollar’s link to gold, ending the system of
fixed exchange rates that had prevailed after WWII, and in February 1973
there was a dollar devaluation. Amidst all these inflationary pressures, the
El Nino event further drove up food prices.
Even further back
n Back in the mid-17th century, there was a period when El Nino events
happened about twice as often on average. This was an unusually turbulent
period of global history that’s been termed the “General Crisis”. Some

historians have proposed that climactic changes such as El Nino played a
role in fomenting instability.
How does an El Nino affect the economy and global
markets: The main transmission channels
A useful way to think about El Nino is a set of different supply shocks via multiple
channels: food prices; energy prices; supply chains; EM balance sheets; and
growing risk premia resulting from the heightened risk of social/political unrest. For
individual economies, the impact depends on their exposure to food in the

consumer basket, and the extent of intervention from policymakers, be that via food
subsidies or tighter monetary policy to counteract inflation.
1. Food: The clearest inflation channel
n Direct supply hit: El Nino shifts rainfall patterns, raising the odds of drought
in parts of Asia and Australia, and flooding in parts of the Americas. This
causes damage to crops, kills livestock, and leads to shipping disruption.
n Emerging market exposure : The impact of a food shock varies

considerably across different economies. In the US for instance, food-at-
home makes up just 8% of the consumer basket for CPI inflation. By
contrast, in several EM economies, over a third of household expenditure is
on food, and an IMF paper2 found that the inflation response to El Nino was
biggest in economies where food made up a higher share of the consumer
basket. So emerging markets like India and Indonesia are among those
more severely affected. Moreover, food inflation is a problematic category

because it’s a necessity, and consumers can’t easily substitute away from
it, as they can with luxury goods.
n Second-round effects: Beyond the immediate impact, El Nino also has
second round effects on inflation. For instance, governments may cushion
the impact of higher food prices with subsidies. That might reduce the
domestic hit temporarily, but by further boosting demand and raising fiscal
deficits, it pushes the adjustment elsewhere and risks creating further

inflation overall.
2. Energy and Hydropower: The less-obvious inflation channel
2 IMF Working Paper: Fair Weather or Foul? The Macroeconomic Effects of El Niño (2015), Cashin,
Mohaddes and Raissi.

### Page 6

20 July 2026
Thematic Research
Page 6 Deutsche Bank AG
n Hydropower shortfall: Lower rainfall in some regions means there’s less
electricity generated from hydro-electric dams. Moreover, the droughts
mean this cut to electricity generation is happening just as heat is raising
demand for cooling, and more water is required for irrigation. So there’s
less supply of energy, just as demand goes up.
n Fuel-switching channel: With hydropower output down, this then forces a

substitution into other forms of energy, such as gas, coal or oil-fired
generation, again raising demand for those other fuels and lifting power
prices.
3. Shipping/supply chains
n Climate effects on supply chains: The last El Nino in 2023-24 showed how
it can impact supply chains, quite aside from a direct impact on the food
supply. For instance, Panama had its third-driest year on record in 2023,
with rainfall around 30% below average, forcing authorities to restrict the

number of ships passing through. This is a point covering around 5% of
global maritime trade.
n Interaction with other chokepoints : Any supply-chain issues are
particularly troublesome today, because the Strait of Hormuz has seen
traffic almost completely stop thanks to the US-Iran conflict. So the more
transit points that are impacted, the more difficult it is for other routes to
absorb any disruption. Indeed, some measures of shipping rates have

already doubled since the start of the year
n Macro Transmission: If key routes are restricted or closed, then that raises
shipping rates further, increases delivery times, and means firms want to
hold more inventories against future disruption. So it’s another negative
supply shock, on top of that seen from higher food and energy prices.

4. EM vulnerability
n Climate impact: By virtue of their geographic position, emerging markets
are more likely to face the impact of flooding or drought associated with El
Nino.
n Household exposure: Emerging market economies have more exposure to

food prices, so any price shock hits them harder, with food making up over
a third of consumer expenditure in several countries.

### Page 7

20 July 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 7
n Policy challenges: This vulnerability makes the policy response to an El
Nino event particularly acute. Central banks might raise rates to keep
inflation expectations in check and defend the currency, but that doesn’t
solve the actual supply shock itself. Moreover, if fiscal policy is used to
cushion the impact, the shock is then shifted onto public balance sheets,
and sovereign risk can become an issue.

Source : USDA Economic Research Service, Our World in Data, Deutsche Bank
5. Social/political unrest
n Higher food prices raise the risk of unrest: Throughout history, higher food
prices have often been correlated with political unrest. Clearly it takes many

factors to spark unrest, but the squeeze on disposable incomes means that
any existing dissatisfaction is magnified. If an El Nino leads to higher food
prices, that in turn makes unrest more likely.
n Recent precedents: In 2010-11, higher food prices were regarded as a
contributing factor behind the Arab Spring. A few years earlier in 2007-08,
there was a wave of instability across many EM countries, including Tunisia,
Egypt, Cameroon, Ivory Coast, and Haiti. And more recently, the 2022 price

shock was a contributor to protests in Ecuador, Sierra Leone, Haiti and
Panama.
n Historical precedents : Looking further back, several of history’s most
important revolutions took place against the backdrop of higher food
prices. The French Revolution of 1789 came after a succession of harvest
failures. The 1848 revolutions in Europe happened at the end of the so-
called “Hungry Forties”, when there was a severe famine after the failure of
potato crops. And in Russia in 1917, food shortages played a key role in

fomenting discontent, with supplies hampered in part by the reallocation of
farm workers into the military for WWI.

What does it mean for economies and markets?
A very strong El Nino event would be another negative supply shock when the
global economy has limited room to absorb one. The combination of higher food

and energy prices will mechanically raise inflation, and broader supply-chain
disruption risks creating further price pressures when the Strait of Hormuz has
already been blocked.
This is particularly problematic, because experience shows that a prolonged period
of above-target inflation raises the risk of inflation expectations becoming
unanchored. That in turn can create a self-fulfilling wage-price spiral, as if firms and
individuals expect high inflation, then they will set their prices and bargain for

wages accordingly.
In several respects, this is reminiscent of the experience in the 1970s. Back then, a
series of seemingly unconnected supply shocks (including an El Nino) occurred that
collectively kept inflation high. Even though each might have been temporary on
paper, implying policy should look through and not over-react, the reality was that
the shocks began to feel permanent because they kept on happening. So inflation
expectations rose and policy eventually had to react.

Emerging markets are particularly vulnerable as well, with the risk greatest in
places where food is a larger part of the consumer basket, and where fiscal space
is more limited to accommodate any shock. Moreover, we know from recent history
that higher food prices are also linked to higher instances of social unrest, creating
further economic uncertainty.
Finally, there is a clear human cost to severe El Nino events. The frequency of natural
disasters goes up and there is a greater risk of famine. Moreover, public health

issues are often correlated with El Nino events, with several channels in operation,
including the heightened risk of cholera after flooding, or the added risk of malaria
from conditions more favourable for mosquitos.
Given the forecasts for the strength of this El Nino, this will be an important event
to look out for over the months ahead.

Document 408: NZ labour market review chartpack, Q2 2026 (2026-08-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/NZ labour market review chartpack, Q2 2026 (2026-08-12).pdf

### Page 1

### Page 2

Westpac New Zealand Economics
2
Michael Gordon, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5670 | michael.gordon@westpac.co.nz      Connect with us
Key points – June quarter 2026
• The June quarter surveys pointed to soft labour market conditions overall, but with some surprises in the details.
• The unemployment rate rose to 5.6% from an upwardly-revised 5.4% in the March quarter. This was higher than we and the market were expecting, and
was the highest reading since September 2015. The broader underutilisation rate also rose, from 12.9 to 13.8%.

• The number of people employed rose by 0.5% for the quarter, stronger than what was suggested by the Monthly Employment Indicator. However, this
was accompanied by an even larger rise in the participation rate from 70.4% to 70.7%.
• Large co-movements in these measures are typically an indicator of sampling error in the survey, and indeed there is other evidence that labour
demand was not as strong as these figures suggest. In these circumstances we generally recommend focusing on the unemployment rate, which is less

vulnerable to sampling error (though not immune).
• We continue to characterise this as a “low-fire, low-hire” labour market. The rate of job losses actually remains below average, as firms have looked to
hold on to workers to the extent that they can. But the flipside is that they have been slow to return to hiring even as activity picks up again. The result is
that, for those who find themselves out of work, it's harder to get in (or get back in) than it has been for decades.

• The Labour Cost Index rose by 0.6% for the quarter, leaving annual growth steady at 2.0%. There is a growing tendency to cite cost-of-living adjustments
as the main reason for pay increases, but at the same time there is less emphasis on retaining or attracting workers.
• For the RBNZ, the messages from these surveys are mixed. The RBNZ looks at a suite of labour market indicators, but they believe the measures with the
most information content are: the unemployment rate (weaker), the vacancy rate (slightly weaker), and the QSBO measure of labour as a constraint on

growth (slightly stronger). Overall there is little to suggest that the economy is running any stronger than what they assumed in their May Monetary Policy
Statement forecasts.

### Page 3

Westpac New Zealand Economics
3
Employment
• The number of people employed rose by 0.5% in the June quarter, according to the
Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS). Employment was up by 1.2% compared to a
year ago.
• The result was stronger than what was suggested by the Monthly Employment Indicator
(MEI), a comprehensive measure that is drawn from income tax data. While there are some
differences in coverage for these two measures, any significant divergence between the two

is most likely to reflect sampling error in the HLFS.
• In contrast, the employer-focused Quarterly Employment Survey (QES) showed a 0.4% fall
in filled jobs and a 0.3% fall in hours paid. The survey is of course subject to sampling error
as well.
• Taking an average of these measures would suggest near-zero growth in employment over
the June quarter. This would be in line with our forecast of very soft growth in economic
activity over the quarter. In addition, the seasonal distortions in the GDP figures mean that

the reported GDP result (due on 17 September) will likely be a negative outcome.

### Page 4

Westpac New Zealand Economics
4
Labour force participation
• The surprising rise in HLFS employment was accompanied by a relatively large rise in the
participation rate from 70.4% to 70.7%.
• Large co-movements in these two measures are not unusual, and again are typically a sign
of sampling error in the survey. In these circumstances we recommend focusing on the
unemployment rate, which is less vulnerable to sampling error (see next page).
• Looking through the quarterly volatility, participation has picked up in recent quarters as

the economy has started to regain momentum. This has been driven by two age groups in
particular: teens and over-60s.
• In the latter case, this reflects the fact that people have a growing propensity to work
beyond the NZ Super age (as there is no penalty for doing so). This age group is also making
up an increasing share of the population. It’s unclear to what extent this can be considered
an increase in the pool of available workers: older workers tend to have low mobility

between jobs, and if they find themselves out of work are more likely to leave the labour
force altogether.
• For teens, participation has picked up over the last year after a fall through 2024-25.
However, while more of them are searching for work, they’re not finding it – a reflection of
the “low-fire, low-hire” jobs market (see next page). The teen unemployment rate is close to
a record high going back to 1986.

### Page 5

Westpac New Zealand Economics
5
Unemployment
• The unemployment rate rose from 5.4% to 5.6% in the June quarter. This was the highest
rate since September 2015.
• The March quarter was revised up from a previously reported 5.3%. This was solely due to
an update of the seasonal adjustment factors (the previous result was already on the margin
between 5.3% and 5.4%).
• The broader underutilisation measure, which includes discouraged jobseekers and part-time

workers wanting more hours, rose sharply from 12.9% to 13.8%. This measure had been
stable in recent quarters even as the unemployment rate had risen.
• The underutilisation rate is typically both higher and more volatile than the unemployment
rate, but both measures are telling broadly the same story of labour market weakness,
reaching levels last seen around 2015.
• We would continue to characterise this as a “low-fire, low-hire” labour market. The rate of

job losses actually remains below average, as firms looked to hold on to workers through the
slowdown. But the flipside is that the need to hire is currently low even as activity picks up
again (see next page). The result is that for those who find themselves out of work, it's harder
to get in (or get back in) than it has been for decades.

### Page 6

Westpac New Zealand Economics
6
Hiring
• Business confidence surveys were showing a strong lift in hiring intentions by the end of
2025, in keeping with the overall improving mood about the economic outlook. However,
the uncertainty generated by the Iran conflict and the sharp rise in fuel costs has dragged
business confidence lower again.
• Job advertisements were up around 7% in the June quarter compared to a year ago. That
said, the level is still some way below pre-Covid levels and well below what we’d associate

with a ‘strong’ labour market, where advertisements will reflect a high degree of churn as
well as new job creation.
• After a solid lift in job advertisements in the March quarter, they fell back a little in the June
quarter. With the labour force growing at the same time, the result was a modest fall in the
vacancy rate, one of the RBNZ’s preferred measures of labour market tightness.
• We estimate that there are around 1.8 people unemployed for every job vacancy, a ratio that

is largely unchanged compared to a year ago.

### Page 7

Westpac New Zealand Economics
7
Population and migration
• The working-age population has grown by around 1.2% over the last year, compared to
a peak of 3.3% growth over 2023. Jobs growth (based on the MEI) is still lagging behind
population growth on an annual basis.
• The pool of potential workers is generally becoming older, with the Baby Boomers
increasingly reaching pension age and beyond, and with migrant arrivals tending to be an
older cohort than we saw pre-Covid – more in their 30s and 40s than their 20s.

• Net migration flows have started to pick up from their lows since late 2025. Departures of
New Zealanders have come down from their highs, though they remain above pre-Covid
levels, as the relative appeal of the Australian jobs market has narrowed.
• Foreign arrivals have picked up to some degree, although the growth appears to have been
mostly driven by the Parent Boost Visa rather than work visas.

### Page 8

Westpac New Zealand Economics
8
Sectoral and regional trends
• Jobs growth has been relatively strong in government-connected sectors such as healthcare
and education, as well as in local and central government administration. Sectors with
connections to international tourism, such as transport and recreational services, have also
seen solid growth.
• Construction and manufacturing sector employment have fallen substantially since late
2023, though there are signs that they have bottomed out in recent months.

• Employment trends have generally been more positive in the South Island than the North,
which likely reflects the greater influence of the agricultural sector – while not a large
employer in itself, farmers have benefited from high export prices and their spending has
flowed into other sectors.
• The rate of unemployment has been broadly steady across the South Island over the last
year, compared to the rise in the North Island. That said, the rise in unemployment over the

June quarter was shared across most regions. (We suspect that the steep rise in Northland
is a rogue reading, which can happen every now and then, especially in regions with
small populations.)

Michael Gordon, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5670 | michael.gordon@westpac.co.nz      Connect with us

### Page 9

Westpac New Zealand Economics
9
Wages
• The Labour Cost Index (LCI) rose by 0.6% in the June quarter. Annual growth remained at
2.0%, the lowest since March 2021. Private sector wages rose by 0.7% for the quarter (2.0%
annual), while the private sector saw a more subdued 0.4% rise (1.7% annual).
• The distribution of pay rates has returned to pre-Covid trends. Only 44% of roles saw a
pay rise over the last year; of those roles that did see an increase, the average size has

converged on the 2-3% range.
• There has been a pickup in the proportion of firms reporting cost-of-living adjustments as
the main reason for pay increases. However, fewer are citing the need to retain or attract
staff. In other words, there has been a shift in the justification for pay rises, rather than an
overall increase in wage pressures.
• The LCI is not a measure of what workers actually receive – it reflects the cost to businesses
for a given unit of labour – and hence shouldn’t be compared to the inflation rate. A more

suitable measure is the analytical unadjusted LCI, which includes pay rises that are related
to productivity gains. This measure was steady at 3.1% on an annual basis, also the lowest
since March 2021.
• Another measure, the QES average hourly earnings, slowed from 3.1% to 2.8% in the year to
June, after having picked up briefly in 2025. This measure is not adjusted for the composition
of jobs and is more volatile than the LCI.

### Page 10

Westpac New Zealand Economics
10
Employment confidence
• The Westpac-McDermott Miller employment confidence survey for the June quarter
showed a marked worsening in households’ perceptions about the jobs market. The
Employment Confidence Index fell by 12.5 points to 83.1, the lowest reading for the survey
since it began in September 2004.
• Perceptions about current job opportunities softened in June, consistent with a renewed
rise in the unemployment rate.

• Workers’ perceptions of their own job security fell sharply, reaching their lowest level since
the Covid lockdown. The fall was greatest among young workers, who in turn had seen the
greatest improvement in previous quarters.
• The fall in confidence was shared across regions, with the North Island tending to see larger
falls than the South Island. This was in keeping with the general tone of economic data
in recent months, with stronger conditions in the South Island providing more of a buffer

against external shocks.
• The survey was held in early June, just before the US-Iran peace agreement. However,
this proved to be a short-lived reprieve, and the fall in world oil prices over June has since
been reversed.

### Page 11

Westpac New Zealand Economics
11
International comparisons
• New Zealand’s unemployment rate is the 19th lowest in the OECD (out of 38 countries).
We had been ranked 18th over the last year or so.
• Notably, New Zealand’s unemployment rate has risen substantially above Australia’s,
whereas historically it has been lower on average. This shift in relative job opportunities has
been a key attractor for New Zealanders over the last couple of years – as it was in the early

2010s when the Australian economy was benefiting from a boom in mining construction.
• New Zealand has the 6th highest employment rate in the OECD (up from 7th last quarter).
We tend to have high rates of labour force participation for 15-64 year olds, and particularly
high participation for the over-65s, as the lack of means testing for NZ Superannuation
means there is no financial disincentive to working beyond pension age.

Document 415: Q2 growth in advanced economies_ resilient despite multiple shocks (2026-07-23)

### Page 1

Paris, 23 July 2026
EcoFlash
Q2 growth in advanced economies: resilient despite multiple shocks
Shocks are mounting, but growth is holding up. Although GDP figures for the Eurozone, France, Germany
and the United States are due to be published on 30 July , our nowcasts indicate that growth returned to its
trend rate in the second quarter. This rate is approximately 1% per annum in the Eurozone, France and
Germany, and 2% per annum in the United States. In Q2, the Eurozone is expected to benefit from sustained

growth in Germany, with investment plans gaining momentum, while France is expected to see a rebound in
exports and residential construction after a poor start in Q1. In the United States, growth is expected to
remain driven by non-residential investment and household consumption but will be held back by strong
imports.
Eurozone: a (fragile) resurgence in momentum. Following a first quarter marred by significant volatility in Irish statistics –
assuming this does not disrupt Q2 GDP figures again – our nowcast points to a rebound in growth of +0.3% q/q in Q2 2026, in

line with our forecast. This nowcast has been recently revised upwards due to improvements in the June PMI surveys and the
(unfortunately temporary) fall in the price of Brent crude. Growth in the Eurozone is expected to be driven by Germany and the
rebound in economic activity in France (see below). The resilience of the Eurozone labour market is likely to continue providing
support for consumption, although household purchasing power is expected to fall by 0.9% q/q in Q2. This decline underscores the

two headwinds facing households: geopolitical uncertainty and the temporary rise in inflation, both of which are expected to continue
to dampen consumption and keep the savings rate at a high level.
Eurozone France
France: a (partial) return to growth. France appears to have returned to growth in the second quarter, with an increase of
+0.2% q/q according to our nowcast. Three factors contributed to the 0.1% q/q decline in Q1: i/ exports: however, Airbus deliveries

have since rebounded sharply (+40% y/y in Q2 after -16% y/y in Q1), and exports have also risen in the steel, IT/electronics and
pharmaceutical sectors; ii/ construction: after a sharp decline across all its sub-sectors in Q1, output is expected to stabilise in Q2
(with a rebound in new housing offsetting ongoing weakness in public works and renovation); 3/ household consumption: this is
expected to remain under pressure in Q2 (following a 0.2% q/q decline in Q1) due to rising inflation, which has prompted households

to revise their purchasing intentions downwards (a decrease of 9 points in June compared with February in the household
confidence survey, 22 points below their historical average).
Germany: the return to growth is being confirmed. Germany is implementing its investment plans (see our analysis) and this
is being reflected in its GDP growth. Our Germany nowcast (which we are publishing for the first time) indicates that growth
reached 0.2% q/q in the second quarter , marking a third consecutive quarter of growth (+0.2% q/q in Q4, +0.3% in Q1) – a

performance not seen since 2022. Public spending is the main driver of this growth, with social spending leading the way (particularly
those associated with an ageing population). Defence spending is also gaining momentum: in Q1 2026, government expenditures
accounted for a quarter of public consumption growth (compared with a tenth a year earlier). This rise in public spending is

### Page 2

supporting public works, with output reaching an all-time high in the first two months of Q2 (+6% q/q). German industry is also
reaping the benefits of this, alongside the resurgence in European demand: domestic industrial orders have risen by 2.6% y/y since
the start of 2026, with an even more significant rise for orders to the Eurozone (+6.2% y/y).
United States: growth held back by inflationary pressures. Survey data are looking strong. The non-manufacturing ISM index

remains high (54 in June, following a peak of 56.1 in February), while the manufacturing ISM index has risen significantly since the
start of the year (averaging 52.9, compared with 47.5 in the second half of 2025). However, GDP growth is unlikely to benefit much
from this uptick in activity, as the rise in imports (particularly of inputs for AI) has also accelerated. This limits the Atlanta Fed’s
nowcast to 1.7% q/q for the second quarter (annualised), while our forecast is slightly more optimistic, at 2.2% annualised

(0.55% q/q, non-annualised). This figure, which is relatively moderate given the high ISM reading, can also be attributed to the
inflationary pressures facing the US economy. These pressures are linked to supply constraints on intermediate goods and the
strength of the labour market and have been exacerbated since March by the rise in oil prices. Consequently, while it is conceivable
that consumption will continue to be driven in Q2 by the wealth effect among affluent households, the confidence and spending of

WESTPAC PACIFIC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC SUMMARY
Papua New Guinea has entered the second half of 2026 in
a considerably stronger position than many expected at the
beginning of the year. The economy continues to benefit from
elevated commodity prices, improving resource sector activity

and ongoing IMF-backed reforms. At the headline level, the
macroeconomic picture is encouraging. Yet beneath these
positive trends, structural weaknesses continue to constrain
broader socio-economic development. This edition explores the
growing disconnect between economic growth and livelihoods.
The impact of the Middle East conflict was largely contained,
with the Government rolling out fuel subsidy packages and
existing zero-rate GST relief measures helping to minimise

the pass-through of higher costs. Higher global commodity
prices also boosted export earnings, supporting the current
account balance. At the time of writing, Brent crude is trading
at US$79/b amid optimism that a US-Iran interim agreement
could reopen the Strait of Hormuz. Sentiment has improved but
remains quite uncertain as more regional countries support de-
escalation efforts and discourage further US military action, led
by Saudi Arabia.
Domestically, the Monetary Policy Committee maintained the

Kina Facility Rate at 5.0% and continues to rely heavily on the
exchange rate as the economy’s primary nominal anchor. The
latest BPNG economic review highlights positive momentum
across both the mineral and non-mineral sectors, supported
by increased production of palm oil, tea, copra and rubber,
alongside continued strength in cocoa and coffee. Government
spending through the Connect PNG programme is also driving
activity in the construction and broader services sectors. Under

the IMF reform programme, BPNG has maintained a crawl-like
exchange rate regime. The kina has continued to depreciate,
although the pace of adjustment has slowed. In 2026 year-to-
date, the exchange rate has moved from 0.2352 to 0.2267, a
decline of around 3.6%. BPNG argues that the kina has moved
closer to its neutral value, but the continued presence of FX
queues and central bank auctions suggests the market has
not yet reached a fully market-clearing level. Meanwhile, the

bid-to-cover ratio at BPNG’s FX auctions has declined from
5.9 times in 2024 to 2.2 in 2026. The entry of three new banks
has also placed upward pressure on deposit rates, although
lending-deposit spreads remain wide.
Formal employment grew by 2.4% in 2025, largely driven by
hiring in the mining and resource sectors. However, formal
employment remains relatively small compared with the size
of PNG’s informal economy. We discuss this further in later

sections, particularly in relation to data gaps failing to capture
informal employment and livelihoods conditions.
PNG LNG exports remain elevated, although production
has softened slightly in 2026. Jan-Jul 2026 export volumes
PNG so far in 2026...
Shamal Chand
Senior Economist, Westpac Pacific Economics

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC PACIFIC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC SUMMARY
... and what lies ahead
were 0.9% below the same period in 2025, while average
utilisation rates were around one percentage point lower. July
utilisation eased to 91.2% following a particularly strong June
reading, suggesting a temporary operational or export-related

slowdown rather than a broader trend. Despite this, LNG export
receipts continue to benefit from strong global prices, with LNG
trading at US$21.18/MMBtu as at 5 August, up 32.4% from
last month and 76.5% higher than a year ago. Despite Taiwan
suspending spot LNG purchases and reassessing imports
following PNG’s closure of Taipei’s office, long-term LNG
contracts remain in place until 2030.
Gold exports to Australia have surged to a new cyclical high.

Exports totalled K8.2bn in Jan-May 2026, up 36.4%, while May
remained above its six-month moving average. April 2026 is
the highest monthly export value on record.
Domestic demand momentum has recovered strongly. The
import indicator averaged +15.9% in Jan-Jun 2026, up from
+3.3% during the same period in 2025 and following a full-year
average growth rate of 13.8% in 2024.
Challenges, risks and the way forward
The 2026 economic outlook faces a mix of opportunities and

risks. An earlier-than-expected final investment decision
(FID) for Papua LNG could boost capital inflows, ease
foreign exchange shortages and support kina convertibility.
Encouragingly, investments through Connect PNG and
upgrades to the Ramu Highway are improving connectivity
between Highland cash-crop producers and coastal export
markets.
The lack of a mature interbank market remains a major hurdle
for monetary sector reforms. In the current setting, BPNG’s Kina

Facility Rate lacks a strong transmission channel to influence
broader market interest rates. While some interbank activity
has emerged, it remains isolated rather than broad-based.
Given that LNG and mining account for the majority of PNG’s
export earnings, any sharp decline in global commodity prices
would reduce government revenues, pressure foreign reserves
and constrain foreign currency availability. If commodity prices
retreat rapidly following a US-Iran agreement, there is a risk

of renewed foreign exchange pressures, particularly if BPNG
moves too quickly on exchange rate liberalisation.
Severe drought cycles can curtail cash-crop production,
including coffee, cocoa and palm oil, while lower river levels
disrupt hydroelectric power generation and inland transport
corridors such as the Fly River copper freight route. We discuss
these risks in greater detail in later sections.
Approaching the 2027 national elections, political pressure

risks driving fiscal slippage. At the same time, persistent
law-and-order challenges, tribal tensions and high youth
unemployment continue to divert fiscal resources towards
policing and security rather than productive capital
development.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
4
WESTPAC PACIFIC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC CHARTS
BPNG’s Monetary Policy Committee kept the KFR unchanged at 5.0%,
with all 5 members voting in favour. However, 2 members preferred a
slower-pace of crawl-like exchange rate arrangement.
Our trade-weighted exchange rate has remained tightly managed

under the crawl-like regime, averaging 76.97 in 2026 YTD, down 6.6%
from 82.39 in 2025, which itself was 6.9% lower than 88.51 in 2024.
PNG LNG exports to Japan stood at K4.49bn in the first-half
of 2026, down by 9.1% compared to K4.94bn in exports in 2025
(January-June).
We have seen growth in the number of Papua New Guineans going
to work in Australia under the PALM scheme, especially those
staying over long-term. As of April, 2,798 were employed.

Global cocoa is trading 31.8% lower than last year, however, it has
picked up by 13.0% since the end of February-2026. Cocoa prices
face upward pressure from the upcoming El Niño event.
Global coffee is trading 9.6% higher than last year, although the
price momentum has been flat since last month. The upcoming
super El Niño is projected to hit major production markets, including
Brazil and Asia, providing upward pressure on prices.

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts are based are reasonable,
